  is determined as of the time of signing and an endorsement by such a
  person does not become unauthorized for the purposes of this Part by
  reason of any subsequent change of circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An endorsement of a security in registered form is
  made when an appropriate person signs, either on the security or on a
  separate document, an assignment or transfer of the security or a
  power to assign or transfer it, or when the signature of an
  appropriate person is written without more on the back of the
  security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special or blank

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An endorsement may be special or in blank.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Blank endorsement

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An endorsement in blank includes an endorsement to
  bearer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special endorsement

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A special endorsement specifies the person to whom
  the security is to be transferred, or who has power to transfer it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of holder

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A holder may convert an endorsement in blank into a
  special endorsement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity of endorser

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise agreed, the endorser assumes no
  obligation that the security will be honoured by the issuer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Partial endorsement

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} An endorsement purporting to be only of part of a
  security representing units intended by the issuer to be separately
  transferable is effective to the extent of the endorsement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure of fiduciary to comply

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Failure of a fiduciary to comply with a controlling
  instrument or act or with the law of the jurisdiction governing the
  fiduciary relationship, including any law requiring the fiduciary to
  obtain court approval of a transfer, does not render the fiduciary's
  endorsement unauthorized for the purposes of this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 65
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 32(E), 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 35(E)
- 2018, c. 8, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-65-20111129.html "Link to previous version of section 65"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of endorsement without delivery

**[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** An endorsement of a
security whether special or in blank does not constitute a transfer
until delivery of the security on which it appears or, if the
endorsement is on a separate document, until delivery of both the
security and that document.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 62
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsement in bearer form

**[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** An endorsement of a
security in bearer form may give notice of an adverse claim under
section 61 but does not otherwise affect any right to registration that
the holder has.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 63
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of unauthorized endorsement

- **[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  owner of a security may assert the ineffectiveness of an endorsement
  against the issuer or any purchaser, other than a purchaser for value
  without notice of an adverse claim who has in good faith received a
  new, reissued or re-registered security on registration of transfer,
  unless the owner

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has ratified an unauthorized endorsement of the
    security; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is otherwise precluded from impugning the
    effectiveness of an unauthorized endorsement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability of issuer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An issuer who registers the transfer of a security on
  an unauthorized endorsement is liable for improper registration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 64
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Warranties of guarantor of signature

- **[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who guarantees a signature of an endorser of a security
  warrants that at the time of signing

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the signature was genuine;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the signer was an appropriate person as defined in
    section 65 to endorse; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the signer had legal capacity to sign.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation of liability

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who guarantees a signature of an endorser
  does not otherwise warrant the rightfulness of the particular
  transfer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Warranties of guarantor of endorsement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who guarantees an endorsement of a security
  warrants both the signature and the rightfulness of the transfer in
  all respects, but an issuer may not require a guarantee of endorsement
  as a condition to registration of transfer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extent of liability

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The warranties referred to in this section are made
  to any person taking or dealing with the security relying on the
  guarantee and the guarantor is liable to such person for any loss
  resulting from breach of warranty.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 65
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Constructive delivery of a security

- **[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Delivery to a purchaser occurs when

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the purchaser or a person designated by the
    purchaser acquires possession of a security;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the broker of the purchaser acquires possession of
    a security specially endorsed to or issued in the name of the
    purchaser;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the broker of the purchaser sends the purchaser
    confirmation of the purchase and identifies in a record a specific
    security as belonging to the purchaser; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} with respect to an identified security to be
    delivered while still in the possession of a third person, that
    person acknowledges holding it for the purchaser.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Constructive ownership

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A purchaser is the owner of a security that a broker
  holds for the purchaser, but is not a holder except in the cases
  referred to in paragraphs (1)(b) and (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ownership of part of fungible bulk

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a security is part of a fungible bulk, a purchaser
  of the security is the owner of a proportionate share in the fungible
  bulk.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to broker

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Notice of an adverse claim received by a broker or by
  a purchaser after the broker takes delivery as a holder for value is
  not effective against the broker or the purchaser, except that, as
  between the broker and the purchaser, the purchaser may demand
  delivery of an equivalent security as to which no notice of an adverse
  claim has been received.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 70
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 36(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-70-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 70"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivery of security

- **[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless
  otherwise agreed, if a sale of a security is made on an exchange or
  otherwise through brokers,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the selling customer fulfils their duty to deliver
    by delivering the security to the selling broker or to a person
    designated by the selling broker or by causing an acknowledgment to
    be made to the selling broker that it is held for the selling
    broker; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the selling broker, including a correspondent
    broker, acting for a selling customer fulfils their duty to deliver
    by delivering the security or a like security to the buying broker
    or to a person designated by the buying broker or by effecting
    clearance of the sale in accordance with the rules of the exchange
    on which the transaction took place.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to deliver

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this section and unless otherwise agreed,
  a transferor's duty to deliver a security under a contract of purchase
  is not fulfilled until the transferor delivers the security in
  negotiable form to the purchaser or to a person designated by the
  purchaser, or causes an acknowledgment to be made to the purchaser
  that the security is held for the purchaser.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivery to broker

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A sale to a broker purchasing for the broker's own
  account is subject to subsection (2) and not subsection (1), unless
  the sale is made on a stock exchange.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 71
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to reclaim possession

- **[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person against whom the transfer of a security is wrongful for any
  reason, including incapacity, may against anyone except a [bona
  fide]{.otherLang lang="la"} purchaser reclaim possession of the
  security or obtain possession of any new security evidencing all or
  part of the same rights or claim damages.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery if unauthorized endorsement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the transfer of a security is wrongful by reason
  of an unauthorized endorsement, the owner may reclaim possession of
  the security or a new security even from a [bona fide]{.otherLang
  lang="la"} purchaser if the ineffectiveness of the purported
  endorsement may be asserted against such purchaser under section 68.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remedies

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The right to reclaim possession of a security may be
  specifically enforced, its transfer may be restrained and the security
  may be impounded or, in Quebec, sequestrated pending litigation.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 72
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 37(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-72-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 72"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to requisites for registration

- **[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless
  otherwise agreed, a transferor shall on demand supply a purchaser with
  proof of authority to transfer or with any other requisite that is
  necessary to obtain registration of the transfer of a security, but if
  the transfer is not for value a transferor need not do so unless the
  purchaser pays the reasonable and necessary costs of the proof and
  transfer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rescission of transfer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the transferor fails to comply with a demand under
  subsection (1) within a reasonable time, the purchaser may reject or
  rescind the transfer.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 73
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seizure of security

**[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** No seizure of a security
or other interest or right evidenced by the security is effective until
the person making the seizure obtains possession of the security.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 74
- 2011, c. 21, s. 38
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-74-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 74"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No liability for acts in good faith

**[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** An agent or mandatary or
a bailee who in good faith, including observance of reasonable
commercial standards if the agent or mandatary or the bailee is in the
business of buying, selling or otherwise dealing with securities of a
corporation has received securities and sold, pledged or delivered them,
according to the instructions of their principal or mandator, is not
liable for conversion or for participation in breach of fiduciary duty
although the principal has no right to dispose of them.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 75
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 33(F), 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 38
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-75-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 75"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Registration]{.HTitleText2} {#h-109437 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to register transfer

- **[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a security in registered form is presented for transfer, the issuer
  shall register the transfer if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the security is endorsed by an appropriate person
    as defined in section 65;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reasonable assurance is given that that endorsement
    is genuine and effective;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the issuer has no duty to inquire into adverse
    claims or has discharged any such duty;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any applicable law relating to the collection of
    taxes has been complied with;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the transfer is rightful or is to a [bona
    fide]{.otherLang lang="la"} purchaser; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any fee referred to in subsection 49(2) has been
    paid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability for delay

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an issuer has a duty to register a transfer of
  a security, the issuer is liable to the person presenting it for
  registration for loss resulting from any unreasonable delay in
  registration or from failure or refusal to register the transfer.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 71
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assurance that endorsement effective

- **[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  issuer may require an assurance that each necessary endorsement on a
  security is genuine and effective by requiring a guarantee of the
  signature of the person endorsing, and by requiring

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the endorsement is by an agent or mandatary,
    reasonable assurance of authority to sign;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the endorsement is by a fiduciary, evidence of
    appointment or incumbency;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if there is more than one fiduciary, reasonable
    assurance that all who are required to sign have done so; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, assurance that corresponds as
    closely as practicable to the foregoing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[guarantee of the
  signature]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), a [[guarantee of
  the signature]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a guarantee signed by or on
  behalf of a person reasonably believed by the issuer to be
  responsible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Standards

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An issuer may adopt reasonable standards to determine
  responsible persons for the purpose of subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[evidence of appointment
  or incumbency]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In paragraph (1)(b), [[evidence of appointment or
  incumbency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a fiduciary appointed by a court, a
    copy of the order certified in accordance with subsection 51(7), and
    dated not earlier than sixty days before the date a security is
    presented for transfer; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, a copy of a document showing the
    appointment or other evidence believed by the issuer to be
    appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Standards

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An issuer may adopt reasonable standards with respect
  to evidence for the purposes of paragraph (4)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No notice to issuer

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An issuer is deemed not to have notice of the
  contents of any document referred to in subsection (4) except to the
  extent that the contents relate directly to appointment or incumbency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice from excess documentation

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If an issuer demands assurance additional to that
  specified in this section for a purpose other than that specified in
  subsection (4) and obtains a copy of a will, trust or partnership
  agreement, by-law or similar document, the issuer is deemed to have
  notice of all matters contained therein affecting the transfer.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 77
- 2011, c. 21, s. 39(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 77"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited duty of inquiry

- **[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  issuer to whom a security is presented for registration has a duty to
  inquire into adverse claims if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} written notice of an adverse claim has been
    received at a time and in a manner that affords the issuer a
    reasonable opportunity to act on it before the issue of a new,
    reissued or re-registered security and the notice discloses the name
    and address of the claimant, the registered owner and the issue of
    which the security is a part; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the issuer is deemed to have notice of an adverse
    claim from a document that it obtained under subsection 77(7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discharge of duty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An issuer may discharge a duty of inquiry by any
  reasonable means, including notifying an adverse claimant by
  registered mail sent to the address furnished by the claimant or, if
  no such address has been furnished, to the claimant's residence or
  regular place of business, that a security has been presented for
  registration of transfer by a named person, and that the transfer will
  be registered unless within thirty days from the date of mailing the
  notice either

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the issuer is served with a restraining order or
    other order of a court; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the issuer is provided with an indemnity bond
    sufficient in the issuer's judgment to protect the issuer and any
    registrar, transfer agent or other agent or mandatary of the issuer
    from any loss that may be incurred by any of them as a result of
    complying with the adverse claim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry into adverse claims

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless an issuer is deemed to have notice of an
  adverse claim from a document that it obtained under subsection 77(7)
  or has received notice of an adverse claim under subsection (1), if a
  security presented for registration is endorsed by the appropriate
  person as defined in section 65, the issuer has no duty to inquire
  into adverse claims, and in particular,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an issuer registering a security in the name of a
    person who is a fiduciary or who is described as a fiduciary is not
    bound to inquire into the existence, extent or correct description
    of the fiduciary relationship and thereafter the issuer may assume
    without inquiry that the newly registered owner continues to be the
    fiduciary until the issuer receives written notice that the
    fiduciary is no longer acting as such with respect to the particular
    security;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an issuer registering transfer on an endorsement by
    a fiduciary has no duty to inquire whether the transfer is made in
    compliance with the document or with the law of the jurisdiction
    governing the fiduciary relationship; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an issuer is deemed not to have notice of the
    contents of any court record or any registered document even if the
    record or document is in the issuer's possession and even if the
    transfer is made on the endorsement of a fiduciary to the fiduciary
    or the fiduciary's nominee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A written notice of adverse claim received by an
  issuer is effective for twelve months from the date when it was
  received unless the notice is renewed in writing.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 78
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 40(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-78-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 78"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation of issuer's liability

- **[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any applicable law
  relating to the collection of taxes, the issuer is not liable to the
  owner or any other person who incurs a loss as a result of the
  registration of a transfer of a security if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the necessary endorsements were on or with the
    security; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the issuer had no duty to inquire into adverse
    claims or had discharged any such duty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of issuer in default

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an issuer has registered a transfer of a security
  to a person not entitled to it, the issuer shall on demand deliver a
  like security to the owner unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subsection (1) applies;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the owner is precluded by subsection 80(1) from
    asserting any claim; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the delivery would result in overissue, in which
    case the issuer's liability is governed by section 52.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 74
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of lost or stolen security

- **[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a security has been lost, apparently destroyed or wrongfully taken,
  and the owner fails to notify the issuer of that fact by giving the
  issuer written notice of an adverse claim within a reasonable time
  after discovering the loss, destruction or taking and if the issuer
  has registered a transfer of the security before receiving such
  notice, the owner is precluded from asserting against the issuer any
  claim to a new security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of issuer to issue a new security

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the owner of a security claims that the
  security has been lost, destroyed or wrongfully taken, the issuer
  shall issue a new security in place of the original security if the
  owner

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} so requests before the issuer has notice that the
    security has been acquired by a [bona fide]{.otherLang lang="la"}
    purchaser;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} furnishes the issuer with a sufficient indemnity
    bond; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} satisfies any other reasonable requirements imposed
    by the issuer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to register transfer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If, after the issue of a new security under
  subsection (2), a [bona fide]{.otherLang lang="la"} purchaser of the
  original security presents the original security for registration of
  transfer, the issuer shall register the transfer unless registration
  would result in overissue, in which case the issuer's liability is
  governed by section 52.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of issuer to recover

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In addition to any rights on an indemnity bond, the
  issuer may recover a new security issued under subsection (2) from the
  person to whom it was issued or anyone taking under the person other
  than a [bona fide]{.otherLang lang="la"} purchaser.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 80
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights and obligations

- **[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  authenticating trustee, registrar, transfer agent or other agent or
  mandatary of an issuer has, in respect of the issue, registration of
  transfer and cancellation of a security of the issuer,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a duty to the issuer to exercise good faith and
    reasonable diligence; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the same obligations to the holder or owner of a
    security and the same rights, privileges and immunities as the
    issuer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to agent or mandatary

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notice to an authenticating trustee, registrar,
  transfer agent or other agent or mandatary of an issuer is notice to
  the issuer with respect to the functions performed by the agent or
  mandatary.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 81
- 2011, c. 21, s. 41
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-81-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 81"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART VIII]{.HLabel1}[Trust Indentures]{.HTitleText1} {#h-109527 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this Part,

  [[event of default]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[event of default]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an event specified
      in a trust indenture on the occurrence of which

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a security interest constituted by the trust
        indenture becomes enforceable, or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the principal, interest and other moneys
        payable thereunder become or may be declared to be payable
        before maturity,

      but the event is not an event of default until all conditions
      prescribed by the trust indenture in connection with such event
      for the giving of notice or the lapse of time or otherwise have
      been satisfied; ([cas de défaut]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[trustee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[trustee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any person appointed as
      trustee, including the administrator of the property of others,
      under the terms of a trust indenture to which a corporation is a
      party and includes any successor
      trustee; ([fiduciaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[trust indenture]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[trust indenture]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any deed, indenture
      or other instrument or act, including any supplement or amendment,
      made by a corporation after its incorporation or continuance under
      this Act, under which the corporation issues debt obligations and
      in which a person is appointed as trustee for the holders of the
      debt obligations. ([acte de fiducie]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This Part applies to a trust indenture if the debt
  obligations issued or to be issued under the trust indenture are part
  of a distribution to the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Director may exempt a trust indenture from this
  Part if the trust indenture, the debt obligations issued thereunder
  and the security interest effected thereby are subject to a law of a
  province or a country other than Canada that is substantially
  equivalent to this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 82
- 2001, c. 14, s. 34(F)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 42
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-82-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 82"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conflict of interest

- **[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall be appointed as trustee if there is a material conflict
  of interest between their role as trustee and their role in any other
  capacity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eliminating conflict of interest

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A trustee shall, within ninety days after becoming
  aware that a material conflict of interest exists

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} eliminate such conflict of interest; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} resign from office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A trust indenture, any debt obligations issued
  thereunder and a security interest effected thereby are valid
  notwithstanding a material conflict of interest of the trustee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of trustee

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a trustee contravenes subsection (1) or (2), any
  interested person may apply to a court for an order that the trustee
  be replaced, and the court may make an order on such terms as it
  thinks fit.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 83
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualification of trustee

**[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** A trustee, or at least
one of the trustees if more than one is appointed, shall be a body
corporate incorporated under the laws of Canada or a province and
authorized to carry on the business of a trust company.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 79
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List of security holders

- **[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  holder of debt obligations issued under a trust indenture may, on
  payment to the trustee of a reasonable fee, require the trustee to
  furnish, within fifteen days after delivering to the trustee the
  statutory declaration referred to in subsection (4), a list setting
  out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the names and addresses of the registered holders
    of the outstanding debt obligations,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the principal amount of outstanding debt
    obligations owned by each such holder, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the aggregate principal amount of debt obligations
    outstanding

  as shown on the records maintained by the trustee on the day that the
  statutory declaration is delivered to that trustee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of issuer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On the demand of a trustee, the issuer of debt
  obligations shall furnish the trustee with the information required to
  enable the trustee to comply with subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corporate applicant

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the person requiring the trustee to furnish a list
  under subsection (1) is a body corporate, the statutory declaration
  required under that subsection shall be made by a director or officer
  of the body corporate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of statutory declaration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The statutory declaration required under
  subsection (1) shall state

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of the person requiring the
    trustee to furnish the list and, if the person is a body corporate,
    the address for service thereof; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the list will not be used except as permitted
    under subsection (5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of list

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A list obtained under this section shall not be used
  by any person except in connection with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an effort to influence the voting of the holders of
    debt obligations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an offer to acquire debt obligations; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any other matter relating to the debt obligations
    or the affairs of the issuer or guarantor thereof.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A person who, without reasonable cause, contravenes
  subsection (5) is guilty of an offence and liable on summary
  conviction to a fine not exceeding five thousand dollars or to
  imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 80
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence of compliance

- **[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  issuer or a guarantor of debt obligations issued or to be issued under
  a trust indenture shall, before doing any act under paragraph (a), (b)
  or (c), furnish the trustee with evidence of compliance with the
  conditions in the trust indenture relating to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the issue, certification and delivery of debt
    obligations under the trust indenture;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the release or release and substitution of property
    subject to a security interest constituted by the trust indenture;
    or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the satisfaction and discharge of the trust
    indenture.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of issuer or guarantor

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On the demand of a trustee, the issuer or guarantor
  of debt obligations issued or to be issued under a trust indenture
  shall furnish the trustee with evidence of compliance with the trust
  indenture by the issuer or guarantor in respect of any act to be done
  by the trustee at the request of the issuer or guarantor.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 81
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of declaration, etc.

**[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** Evidence of compliance as
required by section 86 shall consist of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a statutory declaration or certificate made by a
  director or an officer of the issuer or guarantor stating that the
  conditions referred to in that section have been complied with; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the trust indenture requires compliance with
  conditions that are subject to review

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} by legal counsel, an opinion of legal counsel that
    such conditions have been complied with, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} by an auditor or accountant, an opinion or report
    of the auditor of the issuer or guarantor, or such other accountant
    as the trustee may select, that such conditions have been complied
    with.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 82
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further evidence of compliance

**[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** The evidence of
compliance referred to in section 87 shall include a statement by the
person giving the evidence

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} declaring that they have read and understand the
  conditions of the trust indenture described in section 86;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} describing the nature and scope of the examination or
  investigation on which the certificate, statement or opinion is based;
  and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} declaring that they have made the examination or
  investigation that they believe necessary to enable them to make their
  statements or give their opinions.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 88
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trustee may require evidence of
compliance

- **[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On the
  demand of a trustee, the issuer or guarantor of debt obligations
  issued under a trust indenture shall furnish the trustee with evidence
  in such form as the trustee may require as to compliance with any
  condition thereto relating to any action required or permitted to be
  taken by the issuer or guarantor under the trust indenture.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of compliance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} At least once in each twelve month period beginning
  on the date of the trust indenture and at any other time on the demand
  of a trustee, the issuer or guarantor of debt obligations issued under
  a trust indenture shall furnish the trustee with a certificate that
  the issuer or guarantor has complied with all requirements contained
  in the trust indenture that, if not complied with, would, with the
  giving of notice, lapse of time or otherwise, constitute an event of
  default, or, if there has been failure to so comply, giving
  particulars thereof.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 84
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of default

**[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** The trustee shall give to
the holders of debt obligations issued under a trust indenture, within
thirty days after the trustee becomes aware of the occurrence thereof,
notice of every event of default arising under the trust indenture and
continuing at the time the notice is given, unless the trustee
reasonably believes that it is in the best interests of the holders of
the debt obligations to withhold such notice and so informs the issuer
and guarantor in writing.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 85
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of care

**[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** A trustee in exercising
their powers and discharging their duties shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} act honestly and in good faith with a view to the
  best interests of the holders of the debt obligations issued under the
  trust indenture; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} exercise the care, diligence and skill of a
  reasonably prudent trustee.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 91
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reliance on statements

**[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** Notwithstanding section
91, a trustee is not liable if they rely in good faith on statements
contained in a statutory declaration, certificate, opinion or report
that complies with this Act or the trust indenture.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 92
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No exculpation

**[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** No term of a trust
indenture or of any agreement between a trustee and the holders of debt
obligations issued thereunder or between the trustee and the issuer or
guarantor shall operate so as to relieve a trustee from the duties
imposed on the trustee by section 91.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 93
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

## [PART IX]{.HLabel1}[Receivers, Receiver-managers and Sequestrators]{.HTitleText1} {#h-109634 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Functions of receiver or sequestrator

**[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** A receiver or
sequestrator of any property of a corporation may, subject to the rights
of secured creditors, receive the income from the property, pay the
liabilities connected with the property and realize the security
interest of those on behalf of whom the receiver or sequestrator is
appointed, but, except to the extent permitted by a court, the receiver
or sequestrator may not carry on the business of the corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 94
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 44(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-94-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 94"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Functions of receiver-manager

**[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** A receiver-manager of the
corporation may carry on any business of the corporation to protect the
security interest of those on behalf of whom the receiver-manager is
appointed.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 95
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 45
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-95-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 95"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directors' powers cease

**[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** If a receiver-manager or
sequestrator is appointed by a court or under an instrument or act, the
powers of the directors of the corporation that the receiver-manager or
sequestrator is authorized to exercise may not be exercised by the
directors until the receiver-manager or sequestrator is discharged.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 96
- 2011, c. 21, s. 45
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-96-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 96"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to act

**[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** A receiver,
receiver-manager or sequestrator appointed by a court shall act in
accordance with the directions of the court.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 97
- 2011, c. 21, s. 46(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-97-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 97"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty under instrument or act

**[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** A receiver,
receiver-manager or sequestrator appointed under an instrument or act
shall act in accordance with that instrument or act and any direction of
a court made under section 100.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 98
- 2011, c. 21, s. 46(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-98-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 98"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of care

**[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** A receiver,
receiver-manager or sequestrator of a corporation appointed under an
instrument or act shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} act honestly and in good faith; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} deal with any property of the corporation in their
  possession or control in a commercially reasonable manner.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 99
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 47(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-99-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 99"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions given by court

**[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** On an application by a
receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator, whether appointed by a court
or under an instrument or act, or on an application by any interested
person, a court may make any order it thinks fit including, without
limiting the generality of the foregoing,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an order appointing, replacing or discharging a
  receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator and approving their
  accounts;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order determining the notice to be given to any
  person or dispensing with notice to any person;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} an order fixing the remuneration of the receiver,
  receiver-manager or sequestrator;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} an order requiring the receiver, receiver-manager or
  sequestrator, or a person by or on behalf of whom the receiver,
  receiver-manager or sequestrator is appointed, to make good any
  default in connection with the receiver's, receiver-manager's or
  sequestrator's custody or management of the property and business of
  the corporation, or to relieve any such person from any default on any
  terms that the court thinks fit, and to confirm any act of the
  receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} an order giving directions on any matter relating to
  the duties of the receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 100
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 48(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-100-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 100"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of receiver, receiver-manager or
sequestrator

**[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101 .sectionLabel}** A receiver,
receiver-manager or sequestrator shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} immediately notify the Director of their appointment
  and discharge;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} take into their custody and control the property of
  the corporation in accordance with the court order or instrument or
  act under which they are appointed;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} open and maintain a bank account in their name as
  receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator of the corporation for the
  moneys of the corporation coming under their control;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} keep detailed accounts of all transactions carried
  out as receiver, receiver-manager or sequestrator;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} keep accounts of their administration that shall be
  available during usual business hours for inspection by the directors
  of the corporation;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} prepare at least once in every six month period after
  the date of their appointment financial statements of their
  administration as far as is practicable in the form required by
  section 155; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} on completion of their duties, render a final account
  of their administration in the form adopted for interim accounts under
  paragraph (f).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 101
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 49(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-101-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 101"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART X]{.HLabel1}[Directors and Officers]{.HTitleText1} {#h-109681 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to manage or supervise management

- **[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any unanimous shareholder
  agreement, the directors shall manage, or supervise the management of,
  the business and affairs of a corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Number of directors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A corporation shall have one or more directors but a
  distributing corporation, any of the issued securities of which remain
  outstanding and are held by more than one person, shall have not fewer
  than three directors, at least two of whom are not officers or
  employees of the corporation or its affiliates.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 102
- 2001, c. 14, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By-laws

- **[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the articles, by-laws or a
  unanimous shareholder agreement otherwise provide, the directors may,
  by resolution, make, amend or repeal any by-laws that regulate the
  business or affairs of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shareholder approval

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The directors shall submit a by-law, or an amendment
  or a repeal of a by-law, made under subsection (1) to the shareholders
  at the next meeting of shareholders, and the shareholders may, by
  ordinary resolution, confirm, reject or amend the by-law, amendment or
  repeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A by-law, or an amendment or a repeal of a by-law, is
  effective from the date of the resolution of the directors under
  subsection (1) until it is confirmed, confirmed as amended or rejected
  by the shareholders under subsection (2) or until it ceases to be
  effective under subsection (4) and, where the by-law is confirmed or
  confirmed as amended, it continues in effect in the form in which it
  was so confirmed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a by-law, an amendment or a repeal is rejected by
  the shareholders, or if the directors do not submit a by-law, an
  amendment or a repeal to the shareholders as required under subsection
  (2), the by-law, amendment or repeal ceases to be effective and no
  subsequent resolution of the directors to make, amend or repeal a
  by-law having substantially the same purpose or effect is effective
  until it is confirmed or confirmed as amended by the shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shareholder proposal

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A shareholder entitled to vote at an annual meeting
  of shareholders may, in accordance with section 137, make a proposal
  to make, amend or repeal a by-law.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 103
- 2001, c. 14, s. 36(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Organization meeting

- **[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After issue of the certificate of
  incorporation, a meeting of the directors of the corporation shall be
  held at which the directors may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} make by-laws;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} adopt forms of security certificates and corporate
    records;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} authorize the issue of securities;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} appoint officers;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} appoint an auditor to hold office until the first
    annual meeting of shareholders;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} make banking arrangements; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} transact any other business.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a body corporate to
  which a certificate of amalgamation has been issued under subsection
  185(4) or to which a certificate of continuance has been issued under
  subsection 187(4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calling meeting

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An incorporator or a director may call the meeting of
  directors referred to in subsection (1) by giving not less than five
  days notice thereof by mail to each director, stating the time and
  place of the meeting.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 99
- 1978-79, c. 9, ss. 1(F), 28
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualifications of directors

- **[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following persons are disqualified from being a director of a
  corporation:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} anyone who is less than eighteen years of age;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} anyone who is incapable;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person who is not an individual; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a person who has the status of bankrupt.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further qualifications

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless the articles otherwise provide, a director of
  a corporation is not required to hold shares issued by the
  corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Residency

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3.1), at least twenty-five per
  cent of the directors of a corporation must be resident Canadians.
  However, if a corporation has less than four directors, at least one
  director must be a resident Canadian.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- Canadian ownership or
  control

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} If a corporation engages in an activity in Canada
  in a prescribed business sector or if a corporation, by an Act of
  Parliament or by a regulation made under an Act of Parliament, is
  required, either individually or in order to engage in an activity in
  Canada in a particular business sector, to attain or maintain a
  specified level of Canadian ownership or control, or to restrict, or
  to comply with a restriction in relation to, the number of voting
  shares that any one shareholder may hold, own or control, then a
  majority of the directors of the corporation must be resident
  Canadians.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(3.2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsection (3.1) shall be construed as
  reducing any requirement for a specified number or percentage of
  resident Canadian directors that otherwise applies to a corporation
  referred to in that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If only one or two directors

  [(3.3)]{.lawlabel} If a corporation referred to in subsection (3.1)
  has only one or two directors, that director or one of the two
  directors, as the case may be, must be a resident Canadian.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception for holding corporation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (3.1), not more than one third of
  the directors of a holding corporation referred to in that subsection
  need be resident Canadians if the holding corporation earns in Canada
  directly or through its subsidiaries less than five per cent of the
  gross revenues of the holding corporation and all of its subsidiary
  bodies corporate together as shown in

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the most recent consolidated financial statements
    of the holding corporation referred to in section 157; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the most recent financial statements of the holding
    corporation and its subsidiary bodies corporate as at the end of the
    last completed financial year of the holding corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 105
- 2001, c. 14, s. 37
- 2018, c. 8, s. 12
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-105-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 105"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of directors

- **[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  the time of sending articles of incorporation, the incorporators shall
  send to the Director a notice of directors in the form that the
  Director fixes, and the Director shall file the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of office

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each director named in the notice referred to in
  subsection (1) holds office from the issue of the certificate of
  incorporation until the first meeting of shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Election of directors

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3.1) and paragraph 107(b),
  shareholders of a corporation shall, by ordinary resolution at the
  first meeting of shareholders and at each succeeding annual meeting at
  which an election of directors is required, elect directors to hold
  office for a term ending not later than the close of the third annual
  meeting of shareholders following the election.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Election of directors --- distributing
  corporations

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to paragraph 107(b), shareholders of a
  distributing corporation shall, by ordinary resolution at the first
  meeting of shareholders and at each succeeding annual meeting at which
  an election of directors is required, elect directors to hold office
  for a term ending not later than the close of the next annual meeting
  of shareholders following the election.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions --- certain distributing
  corporations

  [(3.2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (3.1), in the case of any
  prescribed class of distributing corporations or in any prescribed
  circumstances respecting distributing corporations or classes of
  distributing corporations, the directors are to be elected in
  accordance with subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Separate vote for each candidate

  [(3.3)]{.lawlabel} If the election of directors is for a prescribed
  corporation, a separate vote of shareholders shall be taken with
  respect to each candidate nominated for director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Majority voting

  [(3.4)]{.lawlabel} If, at a meeting of shareholders of a distributing
  corporation --- other than in the case of a prescribed class of
  distributing corporations --- at which an election of directors is
  required, there is only one candidate nominated for each position
  available on the board, each candidate is elected only if the number
  of votes cast in their favour represents a majority of the votes cast
  for and against them by the shareholders who are present in person or
  represented by proxy, unless the articles require a greater number of
  votes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staggered terms

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} It is not necessary that all directors elected at a
  meeting of shareholders hold office for the same term.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No stated terms

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A director not elected for an expressly stated term
  ceases to hold office at the close of the first annual meeting of
  shareholders following the director's election.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incumbent directors

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (2), (3) to (3.2) and (5) and
  subject to subsection (6.1), if directors are not elected at a meeting
  of shareholders, the incumbent directors continue in office until
  their successors are elected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incumbent director not elected

  [(6.1)]{.lawlabel} If an incumbent director who was a candidate in an
  election held in accordance with subsection (3.4) was not elected
  during the election, the director may continue in office until the
  earlier of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the 90th day after the day of the election; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which their successor is appointed or
    elected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vacancy

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If, for either of the following reasons, a meeting of
  shareholders fails to elect the number or the minimum number of
  directors required by the articles, the directors elected at that
  meeting may exercise all the powers of the directors if the number of
  directors so elected constitutes a quorum:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a lack of consent, disqualification under
    subsection 105(1) or the death of any candidates; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a lack of a majority referred to in subsection
    (3.4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of directors

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The directors may, unless the articles otherwise
  provide, appoint one or more additional directors, who shall hold
  office for a term ending not later than the close of the next annual
  meeting of shareholders, but the total number of directors so
  appointed shall not exceed one third of the number of directors
  elected at the previous annual meeting of shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(8.1)]{.lawlabel} If an individual who was a candidate in an election
  held in accordance with subsection (3.4) was not elected during that
  election, the individual is not to be appointed, except in prescribed
  circumstances, as a director under subsection (8) or 111(1) before the
  next meeting of shareholders at which an election of directors is
  required.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Election or appointment as director

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} An individual who is elected or appointed to hold
  office as a director is not a director and is deemed not to have been
  elected or appointed to hold office as a director unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} he or she was present at the meeting when the
    election or appointment took place and he or she did not refuse to
    hold office as a director; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} he or she was not present at the meeting when the
    election or appointment took place and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} he or she consented to hold office as a director
      in writing before the election or appointment or within ten days
      after it, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} he or she has acted as a director pursuant to
      the election or appointment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 106
- 1994, c. 24, s. 11
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 38, 135(E)
- [2018, c. 8, s. 13](#1037646-1373824){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1037646-1373824.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-106-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 106"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cumulative voting

**[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** Where the articles
provide for cumulative voting,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the articles shall require a fixed number and not a
  minimum and maximum number of directors;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} each shareholder entitled to vote at an election of
  directors has the right to cast a number of votes equal to the number
  of votes attached to the shares held by the shareholder multiplied by
  the number of directors to be elected, and may cast all of those votes
  in favour of one candidate or distribute them among the candidates in
  any manner;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a separate vote of shareholders shall be taken with
  respect to each candidate nominated for director unless a resolution
  is passed unanimously permitting two or more persons to be elected by
  a single resolution;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if a shareholder has voted for more than one
  candidate without specifying the distribution of votes, the
  shareholder is deemed to have distributed the votes equally among
  those candidates;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} if the number of candidates nominated for director
  exceeds the number of positions to be filled, the candidates who
  receive the least number of votes shall be eliminated until the number
  of candidates remaining equals the number of positions to be filled;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} each director ceases to hold office at the close of
  the first annual meeting of shareholders following the director's
  election;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} a director may be removed from office only if the
  number of votes cast in favour of the director's removal is greater
  than the product of the number of directors required by the articles
  and the number of votes cast against the motion; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the number of directors required by the articles may
  be decreased only if the votes cast in favour of the motion to
  decrease the number of directors is greater than the product of the
  number of directors required by the articles and the number of votes
  cast against the motion.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 107
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 39(E), 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ceasing to hold office

- **[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  director of a corporation ceases to hold office when the director

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} dies or resigns;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is removed in accordance with section 109; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} becomes disqualified under subsection 105(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date of resignation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A resignation of a director becomes effective at the
  time a written resignation is sent to the corporation, or at the time
  specified in the resignation, whichever is later.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 108
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of directors

- **[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to paragraph 107(g), the
  shareholders of a corporation may by ordinary resolution at a special
  meeting remove any director or directors from office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the holders of any class or series of shares of
  a corporation have an exclusive right to elect one or more directors,
  a director so elected may only be removed by an ordinary resolution at
  a meeting of the shareholders of that class or series.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vacancy

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to paragraphs 107(b) to (e), a vacancy
  created by the removal of a director may be filled at the meeting of
  the shareholders at which the director is removed or, if not so
  filled, may be filled under section 111.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resignation (or removal)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If all of the directors have resigned or have been
  removed without replacement, a person who manages or supervises the
  management of the business and affairs of the corporation is deemed to
  be a director for the purposes of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (4) does not apply to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an officer who manages the business or affairs of
    the corporation under the direction or control of a shareholder or
    other person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a lawyer, notary, accountant or other professional
    who participates in the management of the corporation solely for the
    purpose of providing professional services; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a trustee in bankruptcy, receiver,
    receiver-manager, sequestrator or secured creditor who participates
    in the management of the corporation or exercises control over its
    property solely for the purpose of the realization of security or
    the administration of a bankrupt's estate, in the case of a trustee
    in bankruptcy.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 109
- 2001, c. 14, s. 40
- 2011, c. 21, s. 50(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-109-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 109"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attendance at meeting

- **[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  director of a corporation is entitled to receive notice of and to
  attend and be heard at every meeting of shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement of director

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A director who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} resigns,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} receives a notice or otherwise learns of a meeting
    of shareholders called for the purpose of removing the director from
    office, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} receives a notice or otherwise learns of a meeting
    of directors or shareholders at which another person is to be
    appointed or elected to fill the office of director, whether because
    of the director's resignation or removal or because the director's
    term of office has expired or is about to expire,

  is entitled to submit to the corporation a written statement giving
  reasons for resigning or for opposing any proposed action or
  resolution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Circulating statement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A corporation shall forthwith send a copy of the
  statement referred to in subsection (2) to every shareholder entitled
  to receive notice of any meeting referred to in subsection (1) and to
  the Director unless the statement is included in or attached to a
  management proxy circular required by section 150.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No corporation or person acting on its behalf incurs
  any liability by reason only of circulating a director's statement in
  compliance with subsection (3).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 110
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
- 2018, c. 8, s. 13.1(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-110-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 110"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filling vacancy

- **[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection 114(3), but
  subject to subsections (3) and (4), a quorum of directors may fill a
  vacancy among the directors, except a vacancy resulting from an
  increase in the number or the minimum or maximum number of directors
  or a failure to elect the number or minimum number of directors
  provided for in the articles.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calling meeting

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If there is not a quorum of directors or if there has
  been a failure to elect the number or minimum number of directors
  provided for in the articles, the directors then in office shall
  without delay call a special meeting of shareholders to fill the
  vacancy and, if they fail to call a meeting or if there are no
  directors then in office, the meeting may be called by any
  shareholder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class director

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the holders of any class or series of shares of a
  corporation have an exclusive right to elect one or more directors and
  a vacancy occurs among those directors,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (4), the remaining directors
    elected by the holders of that class or series of shares may fill
    the vacancy except a vacancy resulting from an increase in the
    number or the minimum or maximum number of directors for that class
    or series or from a failure to elect the number or minimum number of
    directors provided for in the articles for that class or series; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if there are no remaining directors any holder of
    shares of that class or series may call a meeting of the holders of
    shares of that class or series for the purpose of filling the
    vacancy.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shareholders filling vacancy

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The articles may provide that a vacancy among the
  directors shall only be filled by a vote of the shareholders, or by a
  vote of the holders of any class or series of shares having an
  exclusive right to elect one or more directors if the vacancy occurs
  among the directors elected by that class or series.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unexpired term

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A director appointed or elected to fill a vacancy
  holds office for the unexpired term of their predecessor.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 111
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 41, 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Number of directors

- **[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  shareholders of a corporation may amend the articles to increase or,
  subject to paragraph 107(h), to decrease the number of directors, or
  the minimum or maximum number of directors, but no decrease shall
  shorten the term of an incumbent director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Election of directors where articles
  amended

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the shareholders at a meeting adopt an
  amendment to the articles of a corporation to increase or, subject to
  paragraph 107(h) and to subsection (1), decrease the number or minimum
  or maximum number of directors, the shareholders may, at the meeting,
  elect the number of directors authorized by the amendment, and for
  that purpose, notwithstanding subsections 179(1) and 262(3), on the
  issue of a certificate of amendment the articles are deemed to be
  amended as of the date the shareholders adopt the amendment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 112
- 1994, c. 24, s. 12
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of change of director or
director's address

- **[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  corporation shall, within fifteen days after

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a change is made among its directors, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it receives a notice of change of address of a
    director referred to in subsection (1.1),

  send to the Director a notice, in the form that the Director fixes,
  setting out the change, and the Director shall file the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Director's change of address

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} A director shall, within fifteen days after
  changing his or her address, send the corporation a notice of that
  change.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any interested person, or the Director, may apply to
  a court for an order to require a corporation to comply with
  subsection (1), and the court may so order and make any further order
  it thinks fit.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 113
- 2001, c. 14, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meeting of directors

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the articles or by-laws
  otherwise provide, the directors may meet at any place and on such
  notice as the by-laws require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quorum

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the articles or by-laws, a majority of the
  number of directors or minimum number of directors required by the
  articles constitutes a quorum at any meeting of directors, and,
  notwithstanding any vacancy among the directors, a quorum of directors
  may exercise all the powers of the directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Canadian directors present at meetings

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Directors, other than directors of a corporation
  referred to in subsection 105(4), shall not transact business at a
  meeting of directors unless,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the corporation is subject to subsection 105(3),
    at least twenty-five per cent of the directors present are resident
    Canadians or, if the corporation has less than four directors, at
    least one of the directors present is a resident Canadian; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the corporation is subject to subsection
    105(3.1), a majority of directors present are resident Canadians or
    if the corporation has only two directors, at least one of the
    directors present is a resident Canadian.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (3), directors may transact
  business at a meeting of directors where the number of resident
  Canadian directors, required under that subsection, is not present if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a resident Canadian director who is unable to be
    present approves in writing, or by telephonic, electronic or other
    communication facility, the business transacted at the meeting; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the required number of resident Canadian directors
    would have been present had that director been present at the
    meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of meeting

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A notice of a meeting of directors shall specify any
  matter referred to in subsection 115(3) that is to be dealt with at
  the meeting but, unless the by-laws otherwise provide, need not
  specify the purpose of or the business to be transacted at the
  meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Waiver of notice

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A director may in any manner waive a notice of a
  meeting of directors; and attendance of a director at a meeting of
  directors is a waiver of notice of the meeting, except where a
  director attends a meeting for the express purpose of objecting to the
  transaction of any business on the grounds that the meeting is not
  lawfully called.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjournment

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Notice of an adjourned meeting of directors is not
  required to be given if the time and place of the adjourned meeting is
  announced at the original meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}One director meeting

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Where a corporation has only one director, that
  director may constitute a meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Participation

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the by-laws, a director may, in accordance
  with the regulations, if any, and if all the directors of the
  corporation consent, participate in a meeting of directors or of a
  committee of directors by means of a telephonic, electronic or other
  communication facility that permits all participants to communicate
  adequately with each other during the meeting. A director
  participating in such a meeting by such means is deemed for the
  purposes of this Act to be present at that meeting.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 114
- 2001, c. 14, s. 43
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

- **[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Directors of a corporation may
  appoint from their number a managing director who is a resident
  Canadian or a committee of directors and delegate to such managing
  director or committee any of the powers of the directors.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 14, s. 44\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limits on authority

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (1), no managing director
  and no committee of directors has authority to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} submit to the shareholders any question or matter
    requiring the approval of the shareholders;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} fill a vacancy among the directors or in the office
    of auditor, or appoint additional directors;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} issue securities except as authorized by the
    directors;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} issue shares of a series under section 27 except
    as authorized by the directors;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} declare dividends;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} purchase, redeem or otherwise acquire shares issued
    by the corporation;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} pay a commission referred to in section 41 except
    as authorized by the directors;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} approve a management proxy circular referred to in
    Part XIII;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} approve a take-over bid circular or directors'
    circular referred to in Part XVII;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} approve any financial statements referred to in
    section 155; or

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} adopt, amend or repeal by-laws.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 115
- 2001, c. 14, s. 44
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity of acts of directors and
officers

**[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** An act of a director or
officer is valid notwithstanding an irregularity in their election or
appointment or a defect in their qualification.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 116
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resolution in lieu of meeting

- **[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  resolution in writing, signed by all the directors entitled to vote on
  that resolution at a meeting of directors or committee of directors,
  is as valid as if it had been passed at a meeting of directors or
  committee of directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing resolution

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A copy of every resolution referred to in
  subsection (1) shall be kept with the minutes of the proceedings of
  the directors or committee of directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless a ballot is demanded, an entry in the minutes
  of a meeting to the effect that the chairperson of the meeting
  declared a resolution to be carried or defeated is, in the absence of
  evidence to the contrary, proof of the fact without proof of the
  number or proportion of the votes recorded in favour of or against the
  resolution.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 117
- 2001, c. 14, s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directors' liability

- **[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Directors of a corporation who vote
  for or consent to a resolution authorizing the issue of a share under
  section 25 for a consideration other than money are jointly and
  severally, or solidarily, liable to the corporation to make good any
  amount by which the consideration received is less than the fair
  equivalent of the money that the corporation would have received if
  the share had been issued for money on the date of the resolution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further directors' liabilities

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Directors of a corporation who vote for or consent to
  a resolution authorizing any of the following are jointly and
  severally, or solidarily, liable to restore to the corporation any
  amounts so distributed or paid and not otherwise recovered by the
  corporation:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a purchase, redemption or other acquisition of
    shares contrary to section 34, 35 or 36;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a commission contrary to section 41;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a payment of a dividend contrary to section 42;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a payment of an indemnity contrary to section 124;
    or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a payment to a shareholder contrary to section 190
    or 241.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contribution

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A director who has satisfied a judgment rendered
  under this section is entitled to contribution from the other
  directors who voted for or consented to the unlawful act on which the
  judgment was founded.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A director liable under subsection (2) is entitled to
  apply to a court for an order compelling a shareholder or other
  recipient to pay or deliver to the director any money or property that
  was paid or distributed to the shareholder or other recipient contrary
  to section 34, 35, 36, 41, 42, 124, 190 or 241.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order of court

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In connection with an application under
  subsection (4) a court may, if it is satisfied that it is equitable to
  do so,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} order a shareholder or other recipient to pay or
    deliver to a director any money or property that was paid or
    distributed to the shareholder or other recipient contrary to
    section 34, 35, 36, 41, 42, 124, 190 or 241;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} order a corporation to return or issue shares to a
    person from whom the corporation has purchased, redeemed or
    otherwise acquired shares; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} make any further order it thinks fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No liability

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A director who proves that the director did not know
  and could not reasonably have known that the share was issued for a
  consideration less than the fair equivalent of the money that the
  corporation would have received if the share had been issued for money
  is not liable under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An action to enforce a liability imposed by this
  section may not be commenced after two years from the date of the
  resolution authorizing the action complained of.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 118
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 46, 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability of directors for wages
