- **[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Directors of a corporation are
  jointly and severally, or solidarily, liable to employees of the
  corporation for all debts not exceeding six months wages payable to
  each such employee for services performed for the corporation while
  they are such directors respectively.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions precedent to liability

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A director is not liable under subsection (1) unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the corporation has been sued for the debt within
    six months after it has become due and execution has been returned
    unsatisfied in whole or in part;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the corporation has commenced liquidation and
    dissolution proceedings or has been dissolved and a claim for the
    debt has been proved within six months after the earlier of the date
    of commencement of the liquidation and dissolution proceedings and
    the date of dissolution; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the corporation has made an assignment or a
    bankruptcy order has been made against it under the [Bankruptcy and
    Insolvency Act](/eng/acts/B-3) and a claim for the debt has been
    proved within six months after the date of the assignment or
    bankruptcy order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A director, unless sued for a debt referred to in
  subsection (1) while a director or within two years after ceasing to
  be a director, is not liable under this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amount due after execution

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where execution referred to in paragraph (2)(a) has
  issued, the amount recoverable from a director is the amount remaining
  unsatisfied after execution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subrogation of director

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A director who pays a debt referred to in
  subsection (1) that is proved in liquidation and dissolution or
  bankruptcy proceedings is entitled to any priority that the employee
  would have been entitled to and, if a judgment has been obtained, the
  director is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in Quebec, subrogated to the employee's rights as
    declared in the judgment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} elsewhere in Canada, entitled to an assignment of
    the judgment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contribution

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A director who has satisfied a claim under this
  section is entitled to contribution from the other directors who were
  liable for the claim.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 119
- 1992, c. 27, s. 90
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 47, 135(E)
- 2004, c. 25, s. 187
- 2011, c. 21, s. 51
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-119-20041215.html "Link to previous version of section 119"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of interest

- **[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  director or an officer of a corporation shall disclose to the
  corporation, in writing or by requesting to have it entered in the
  minutes of meetings of directors or of meetings of committees of
  directors, the nature and extent of any interest that he or she has in
  a material contract or material transaction, whether made or proposed,
  with the corporation, if the director or officer

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is a party to the contract or transaction;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is a director or an officer, or an individual
    acting in a similar capacity, of a party to the contract or
    transaction; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has a material interest in a party to the contract
    or transaction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of disclosure for director

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The disclosure required by subsection (1) shall be
  made, in the case of a director,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at the meeting at which a proposed contract or
    transaction is first considered;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the director was not, at the time of the meeting
    referred to in paragraph (a), interested in a proposed contract or
    transaction, at the first meeting after he or she becomes so
    interested;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the director becomes interested after a contract
    or transaction is made, at the first meeting after he or she becomes
    so interested; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if an individual who is interested in a contract or
    transaction later becomes a director, at the first meeting after he
    or she becomes a director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of disclosure for officer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The disclosure required by subsection (1) shall be
  made, in the case of an officer who is not a director,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} immediately after he or she becomes aware that the
    contract, transaction, proposed contract or proposed transaction is
    to be considered or has been considered at a meeting;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the officer becomes interested after a contract
    or transaction is made, immediately after he or she becomes so
    interested; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if an individual who is interested in a contract
    later becomes an officer, immediately after he or she becomes an
    officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of disclosure for director or
  officer

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a material contract or material transaction,
  whether entered into or proposed, is one that, in the ordinary course
  of the corporation's business, would not require approval by the
  directors or shareholders, a director or officer shall disclose, in
  writing to the corporation or request to have it entered in the
  minutes of meetings of directors or of meetings of committees of
  directors, the nature and extent of his or her interest immediately
  after he or she becomes aware of the contract or transaction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voting

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A director required to make a disclosure under
  subsection (1) shall not vote on any resolution to approve the
  contract or transaction unless the contract or transaction

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} relates primarily to his or her remuneration as a
    director, officer, employee, agent or mandatary of the corporation
    or an affiliate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is for indemnity or insurance under section 124; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is with an affiliate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing disclosure

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, a general notice to
  the directors declaring that a director or an officer is to be
  regarded as interested, for any of the following reasons, in a
  contract or transaction made with a party, is a sufficient declaration
  of interest in relation to the contract or transaction:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the director or officer is a director or officer,
    or acting in a similar capacity, of a party referred to in paragraph
    (1)(b) or (c);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the director or officer has a material interest in
    the party; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} there has been a material change in the nature of
    the director's or the officer's interest in the party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to disclosures

  [(6.1)]{.lawlabel} The shareholders of the corporation may examine the
  portions of any minutes of meetings of directors or of committees of
  directors that contain disclosures under this section, and any other
  documents that contain those disclosures, during the usual business
  hours of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Avoidance standards

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A contract or transaction for which disclosure is
  required under subsection (1) is not invalid, and the director or
  officer is not accountable to the corporation or its shareholders for
  any profit realized from the contract or transaction, because of the
  director's or officer's interest in the contract or transaction or
  because the director was present or was counted to determine whether a
  quorum existed at the meeting of directors or committee of directors
  that considered the contract or transaction, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} disclosure of the interest was made in accordance
    with subsections (1) to (6);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the directors approved the contract or transaction;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the contract or transaction was reasonable and fair
    to the corporation when it was approved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confirmation by shareholders

  [(7.1)]{.lawlabel} Even if the conditions of subsection (7) are not
  met, a director or officer, acting honestly and in good faith, is not
  accountable to the corporation or to its shareholders for any profit
  realized from a contract or transaction for which disclosure is
  required under subsection (1), and the contract or transaction is not
  invalid by reason only of the interest of the director or officer in
  the contract or transaction, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the contract or transaction is approved or
    confirmed by special resolution at a meeting of the shareholders;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} disclosure of the interest was made to the
    shareholders in a manner sufficient to indicate its nature before
    the contract or transaction was approved or confirmed; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the contract or transaction was reasonable and fair
    to the corporation when it was approved or confirmed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to court

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If a director or an officer of a corporation fails to
  comply with this section, a court may, on application of the
  corporation or any of its shareholders, set aside the contract or
  transaction on any terms that it thinks fit, or require the director
  or officer to account to the corporation for any profit or gain
  realized on it, or do both those things.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 120
- 2001, c. 14, s. 48
- 2011, c. 21, s. 52(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-120-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 120"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officers

**[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** Subject to the
articles, the by-laws or any unanimous shareholder agreement,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the directors may designate the offices of the
  corporation, appoint as officers persons of full capacity, specify
  their duties and delegate to them powers to manage the business and
  affairs of the corporation, except powers to do anything referred to
  in subsection 115(3);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a director may be appointed to any office of the
  corporation; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} two or more offices of the corporation may be held by
  the same person.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 121
- 2001, c. 14, s. 49(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of care of directors and officers

- **[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every director and officer of a
  corporation in exercising their powers and discharging their duties
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} act honestly and in good faith with a view to the
    best interests of the corporation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} exercise the care, diligence and skill that a
    reasonably prudent person would exercise in comparable
    circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Best interests of the corporation

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} When acting with a view to the best interests of
  the corporation under paragraph (1)(a), the directors and officers of
  the corporation may consider, but are not limited to, the following
  factors:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the interests of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} shareholders,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} employees,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} retirees and pensioners,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} creditors,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} consumers, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} governments;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the environment; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the long-term interests of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to comply

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every director and officer of a corporation shall
  comply with this Act, the regulations, articles, by-laws and any
  unanimous shareholder agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No exculpation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 146(5), no provision in a
  contract, the articles, the by-laws or a resolution relieves a
  director or officer from the duty to act in accordance with this Act
  or the regulations or relieves them from liability for a breach
  thereof.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 122
- 1994, c. 24, s. 13(F)
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
- [2019, c. 29, s. 141](#1155915-1173346){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1155915-1173346.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-122-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 122"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissent

- **[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  director who is present at a meeting of directors or committee of
  directors is deemed to have consented to any resolution passed or
  action taken at the meeting unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the director requests a dissent to be entered in
    the minutes of the meeting, or the dissent has been entered in the
    minutes;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the director sends a written dissent to the
    secretary of the meeting before the meeting is adjourned; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the director sends a dissent by registered mail or
    delivers it to the registered office of the corporation immediately
    after the meeting is adjourned.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loss of right to dissent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A director who votes for or consents to a resolution
  is not entitled to dissent under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissent of absent director

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A director who was not present at a meeting at which
  a resolution was passed or action taken is deemed to have consented
  thereto unless within seven days after becoming aware of the
  resolution, the director aware or the resolution, the director

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} causes a dissent to be placed with the minutes of
    the meeting; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sends a dissent by registered mail or delivers it
    to the registered office of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defence --- reasonable diligence

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A director is not liable under section 118 or 119,
  and has complied with his or her duties under subsection 122(2), if
  the director exercised the care, diligence and skill that a reasonably
  prudent person would have exercised in comparable circumstances,
  including reliance in good faith on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} financial statements of the corporation represented
    to the director by an officer of the corporation or in a written
    report of the auditor of the corporation fairly to reflect the
    financial condition of the corporation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a report of a person whose profession lends
    credibility to a statement made by the professional person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defence --- good faith

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A director has complied with his or her duties under
  subsection 122(1) if the director relied in good faith on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} financial statements of the corporation represented
    to the director by an officer of the corporation or in a written
    report of the auditor of the corporation fairly to reflect the
    financial condition of the corporation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a report of a person whose profession lends
    credibility to a statement made by the professional person.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 123
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 50, 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Indemnification

- **[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  corporation may indemnify a director or officer of the corporation, a
  former director or officer of the corporation or another individual
  who acts or acted at the corporation's request as a director or
  officer, or an individual acting in a similar capacity, of another
  entity, against all costs, charges and expenses, including an amount
  paid to settle an action or satisfy a judgment, reasonably incurred by
  the individual in respect of any civil, criminal, administrative,
  investigative or other proceeding in which the individual is involved
  because of that association with the corporation or other entity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advance of costs

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A corporation may advance moneys to a director,
  officer or other individual for the costs, charges and expenses of a
  proceeding referred to in subsection (1). The individual shall repay
  the moneys if the individual does not fulfil the conditions of
  subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A corporation may not indemnify an individual under
  subsection (1) unless the individual

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} acted honestly and in good faith with a view to the
    best interests of the corporation, or, as the case may be, to the
    best interests of the other entity for which the individual acted as
    director or officer or in a similar capacity at the corporation's
    request; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a criminal or administrative action
    or proceeding that is enforced by a monetary penalty, the individual
    had reasonable grounds for believing that the individual's conduct
    was lawful.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Indemnification in derivative actions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A corporation may with the approval of a court,
  indemnify an individual referred to in subsection (1), or advance
  moneys under subsection (2), in respect of an action by or on behalf
  of the corporation or other entity to procure a judgment in its
  favour, to which the individual is made a party because of the
  individual's association with the corporation or other entity as
  described in subsection (1) against all costs, charges and expenses
  reasonably incurred by the individual in connection with such action,
  if the individual fulfils the conditions set out in subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to indemnity

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), an individual referred to in
  that subsection is entitled to indemnity from the corporation in
  respect of all costs, charges and expenses reasonably incurred by the
  individual in connection with the defence of any civil, criminal,
  administrative, investigative or other proceeding to which the
  individual is subject because of the individual's association with the
  corporation or other entity as described in subsection (1), if the
  individual seeking indemnity

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} was not judged by the court or other competent
    authority to have committed any fault or omitted to do anything that
    the individual ought to have done; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} fulfils the conditions set out in subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Insurance

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A corporation may purchase and maintain insurance for
  the benefit of an individual referred to in subsection (1) against any
  liability incurred by the individual

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the individual's capacity as a director or
    officer of the corporation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the individual's capacity as a director or
    officer, or similar capacity, of another entity, if the individual
    acts or acted in that capacity at the corporation's request.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to court

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A corporation, an individual or an entity referred to
  in subsection (1) may apply to a court for an order approving an
  indemnity under this section and the court may so order and make any
  further order that it sees fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Director

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} An applicant under subsection (7) shall give the
  Director notice of the application and the Director is entitled to
  appear and be heard in person or by counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other notice

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} On an application under subsection (7) the court may
  order notice to be given to any interested person and the person is
  entitled to appear and be heard in person or by counsel.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 124
- 2001, c. 14, s. 51
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration

**[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** Subject to the
articles, the by-laws or any unanimous shareholder agreement, the
directors of a corporation may fix the remuneration of the directors,
officers and employees of the corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 120
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

## [PART XI]{.HLabel1}[Insider Trading]{.HTitleText1} {#h-110071 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this Part,

  [[business combination]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[business combination]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an acquisition
      of all or substantially all the property of one body corporate by
      another, or an amalgamation of two or more bodies corporate, or
      any similar reorganization between or among two or more bodies
      corporate; ([regroupement d'entreprises]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[distributing corporation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[distributing corporation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
      2001, c. 14, s. 52\]]{.Repealed}

  [[insider]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[insider]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, except in section 131,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a director or officer of a distributing
        corporation;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a director or officer of a subsidiary of a
        distributing corporation;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a director or officer of a body corporate that
        enters into a business combination with a distributing
        corporation; and

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a person employed or retained by a distributing
        corporation; ([initié]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the chairperson of the board
      of directors, the president, a vice-president, the secretary, the
      treasurer, the comptroller, the general counsel, the general
      manager, a managing director, of an entity, or any other
      individual who performs functions for an entity similar to those
      normally performed by an individual occupying any of those
      offices; ([dirigeant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[share]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[share]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a share carrying voting rights
      under all circumstances or by reason of the occurrence of an event
      that has occurred and that is continuing, and includes

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a security currently convertible into such a
        share, and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} currently exercisable options and rights to
        acquire such a share or such a convertible
        security. ([action]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further interpretation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Part,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a director or an officer of a body corporate that
    beneficially owns, directly or indirectly, shares of a distributing
    corporation, or that exercises control or direction over shares of
    the distributing corporation, or that has a combination of any such
    ownership, control and direction, carrying more than the prescribed
    percentage of voting rights attached to all of the outstanding
    shares of the distributing corporation not including shares held by
    the body corporate as underwriter while those shares are in the
    course of a distribution to the public is deemed to be an insider of
    the distributing corporation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a director or an officer of a body corporate that
    is a subsidiary is deemed to be an insider of its holding
    distributing corporation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person is deemed to beneficially own shares that
    are beneficially owned by a body corporate controlled directly or
    indirectly by the person;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a body corporate is deemed to own beneficially
    shares beneficially owned by its affiliates; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the acquisition or disposition by an insider of an
    option or right to acquire a share is deemed to be a change in the
    beneficial ownership of the share to which the option or right to
    acquire relates.

- [(3) and (4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 14, s. 52\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 126
- 1994, c. 24, s. 14(F)
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 52, 135(E)
- 2018, c. 8, s. 14(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-126-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 126"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[127 to 129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127_to_129
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 14, s. 53\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition of short sale

- **[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  insider shall not knowingly sell, directly or indirectly, a security
  of a distributing corporation or any of its affiliates if the insider
  selling the security does not own or has not fully paid for the
  security to be sold.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calls and puts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An insider shall not knowingly, directly or
  indirectly, sell a call or buy a put in respect of a security of the
  corporation or any of its affiliates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), an insider may sell a
  security they do not own if they own another security convertible into
  the security sold or an option or right to acquire the security sold
  and, within ten days after the sale, they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} exercise the conversion privilege, option or right
    and deliver the security so acquired to the purchaser; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} transfer the convertible security, option or right
    to the purchaser.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An insider who contravenes subsection (1) or (2) is
  guilty of an offence and liable on summary conviction to a fine not
  exceeding the greater of one million dollars and three times the
  profit made, or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months or
  to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 130
- 2001, c. 14, s. 54
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[insider]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, with respect to a
  corporation,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the corporation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an affiliate of the corporation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a director or an officer of the corporation or of
    any person described in paragraph (b), (d) or (f);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a person who beneficially owns, directly or
    indirectly, shares of the corporation or who exercises control or
    direction over shares of the corporation, or who has a combination
    of any such ownership, control and direction, carrying more than the
    prescribed percentage of voting rights attached to all of the
    outstanding shares of the corporation not including shares held by
    the person as underwriter while those shares are in the course of a
    distribution to the public;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a person, other than a person described in
    paragraph (f), employed or retained by the corporation or by a
    person described in paragraph (f);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a person who engages in or proposes to engage in
    any business or professional activity with or on behalf of the
    corporation;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a person who received, while they were a person
    described in any of paragraphs (a) to (f), material confidential
    information concerning the corporation;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a person who receives material confidential
    information from a person described in this subsection or in
    subsection (3) or (3.1), including a person described in this
    paragraph, and who knows or who ought reasonably to have known that
    the person giving the information is a person described in this
    subsection or in subsection (3) or (3.1), including a person
    described in this paragraph; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a prescribed person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expanded definition of
  [[security]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, the following are
  deemed to be a security of the corporation:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a put, call, option or other right or obligation to
    purchase or sell a security of the corporation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a security of another entity, the market price of
    which varies materially with the market price of the securities of
    the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed insiders

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, a person who
  proposes to make a take-over bid (as defined in the regulations) for
  securities of a corporation, or to enter into a business combination
  with a corporation, is an insider of the corporation with respect to
  material confidential information obtained from the corporation and is
  an insider of the corporation for the purposes of subsection (6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed insiders

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} An insider of a person referred to in subsection
  (3), and an affiliate or associate of such a person, is an insider of
  the corporation referred to in that subsection. Paragraphs (1)(b)
  to (i) apply in determining whether a person is such an insider except
  that references to "corporation" in those paragraphs are to be read as
  references to "person described in subsection (3)".

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Insider trading --- compensation to
  persons

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An insider who purchases or sells a security of the
  corporation with knowledge of confidential information that, if
  generally known, might reasonably be expected to affect materially the
  value of any of the securities of the corporation is liable to
  compensate the seller of the security or the purchaser of the
  security, as the case may be, for any damages suffered by the seller
  or purchaser as a result of the purchase or sale, unless the insider
  establishes that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the insider reasonably believed that the
    information had been generally disclosed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the information was known, or ought reasonably to
    have been known, by the seller or purchaser; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the purchase or sale of the security took place in
    the prescribed circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Insider trading --- compensation to
  corporation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The insider is accountable to the corporation for any
  benefit or advantage received or receivable by the insider as a result
  of a purchase or sale described in subsection (4) unless the insider
  establishes the circumstances described in paragraph (4)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tipping --- compensation to persons

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An insider of the corporation who discloses to
  another person confidential information with respect to the
  corporation that has not been generally disclosed and that, if
  generally known, might reasonably be expected to affect materially the
  value of any of the securities of the corporation is liable to
  compensate for damages any person who subsequently sells securities of
  the corporation to, or purchases securities of the corporation from,
  any person that received the information, unless the insider
  establishes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that the insider reasonably believed that the
    information had been generally disclosed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the information was known, or ought reasonably
    to have been known, by the person who alleges to have suffered the
    damages;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that the disclosure of the information was
    necessary in the course of the business of the insider, except if
    the insider is a person described in subsection (3) or (3.1); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the insider is a person described in
    subsection (3) or (3.1), that the disclosure of the information was
    necessary to effect the take-over bid or the business combination,
    as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tipping --- compensation to corporation

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The insider is accountable to the corporation for any
  benefit or advantage received or receivable by the insider as a result
  of a disclosure of the information as described in subsection (6)
  unless the insider establishes the circumstances described in
  paragraph (6)(a), (c) or (d).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measure of damages

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The court may assess damages under subsection (4)
  or (6) in accordance with any measure of damages that it considers
  relevant in the circumstances. However, in assessing damages in a
  situation involving a security of a distributing corporation, the
  court must consider the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the plaintiff is a purchaser, the price paid by
    the plaintiff for the security less the average market price of the
    security over the twenty trading days immediately following general
    disclosure of the information; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the plaintiff is a seller, the average market
    price of the security over the twenty trading days immediately
    following general disclosure of the information, less the price that
    the plaintiff received for the security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If more than one insider is liable under
  subsection (4) or (6) with respect to the same transaction or series
  of transactions, their liability is joint and several, or solidary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} An action to enforce a right created by
  subsections (4) to (7) may be commenced only within two years after
  discovery of the facts that gave rise to the cause of action.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 131
- 2001, c. 14, s. 54
:::

## [PART XII]{.HLabel1}[Shareholders]{.HTitleText1} {#h-110155 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Place of meetings

- **[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Meetings of shareholders of a
  corporation shall be held at the place within Canada provided in the
  by-laws or, in the absence of such provision, at the place within
  Canada that the directors determine.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meeting outside Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a meeting of shareholders of
  a corporation may be held at a place outside Canada if the place is
  specified in the articles or all the shareholders entitled to vote at
  the meeting agree that the meeting is to be held at that place.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A shareholder who attends a meeting of shareholders
  held outside Canada is deemed to have agreed to it being held outside
  Canada except when the shareholder attends the meeting for the express
  purpose of objecting to the transaction of any business on the grounds
  that the meeting is not lawfully held.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Participation in meeting by electronic
  means

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Unless the by-laws otherwise provide, any person
  entitled to attend a meeting of shareholders may participate in the
  meeting, in accordance with the regulations, if any, by means of a
  telephonic, electronic or other communication facility that permits
  all participants to communicate adequately with each other during the
  meeting, if the corporation makes available such a communication
  facility. A person participating in a meeting by such means is deemed
  for the purposes of this Act to be present at the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meeting held by electronic means

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the directors or the shareholders of a corporation
  call a meeting of shareholders pursuant to this Act, those directors
  or shareholders, as the case may be, may determine that the meeting
  shall be held, in accordance with the regulations, if any, entirely by
  means of a telephonic, electronic or other communication facility that
  permits all participants to communicate adequately with each other
  during the meeting, if the by-laws so provide.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 132
- 2001, c. 14, s. 55
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calling annual meetings

- **[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  directors of a corporation shall call an annual meeting of
  shareholders

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} not later than eighteen months after the
    corporation comes into existence; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subsequently, not later than fifteen months after
    holding the last preceding annual meeting but no later than six
    months after the end of the corporation's preceding financial year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calling special meetings

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The directors of a corporation may at any time call a
  special meeting of shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to delay calling of annual
  meeting

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the corporation may apply to
  the court for an order extending the time for calling an annual
  meeting.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 133
- 2001, c. 14, s. 56
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fixing record date

- **[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  directors may, within the prescribed period, fix in advance a date as
  the record date for the purpose of determining shareholders

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} entitled to receive payment of a dividend;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} entitled to participate in a liquidation
    distribution;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} entitled to receive notice of a meeting of
    shareholders;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} entitled to vote at a meeting of shareholders; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} for any other purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No record date fixed

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If no record date is fixed,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the record date for the determination of
    shareholders entitled to receive notice of a meeting of shareholders
    shall be

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at the close of business on the day immediately
      preceding the day on which the notice is given, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if no notice is given, the day on which the
      meeting is held; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the record date for the determination of
    shareholders for any purpose other than to establish a shareholder's
    right to receive notice of a meeting or to vote shall be at the
    close of business on the day on which the directors pass the
    resolution relating thereto.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When record date fixed

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a record date is fixed, unless notice of the
  record date is waived in writing by every holder of a share of the
  class or series affected whose name is set out in the securities
  register at the close of business on the day the directors fix the
  record date, notice of the record date must be given within the
  prescribed period

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by advertisement in a newspaper published or
    distributed in the place where the corporation has its registered
    office and in each place in Canada where it has a transfer agent or
    where a transfer of its shares may be recorded; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by written notice to each stock exchange in Canada
    on which the shares of the corporation are listed for trading.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 14, s. 57\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 134
- 2001, c. 14, s. 57
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of meeting

- **[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notice of the time and place of a
  meeting of shareholders shall be sent within the prescribed period to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} each shareholder entitled to vote at the meeting;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} each director; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the auditor of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- not a distributing
  corporation

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a corporation that is not a
  distributing corporation, the notice may be sent within a shorter
  period if so specified in the articles or by-laws.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- shareholders not
  registered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice of a meeting is not required to be sent to
  shareholders who were not registered on the records of the corporation
  or its transfer agent on the record date determined under paragraph
  134(1)(c) or subsection 134(2), but failure to receive a notice does
  not deprive a shareholder of the right to vote at the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjournment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a meeting of shareholders is adjourned for less
  than thirty days it is not necessary, unless the by-laws otherwise
  provide, to give notice of the adjourned meeting, other than by
  announcement at the earliest meeting that is adjourned.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of adjourned meeting

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a meeting of shareholders is adjourned by one or
  more adjournments for an aggregate of thirty days or more, notice of
  the adjourned meeting shall be given as for an original meeting but,
  unless the meeting is adjourned by one or more adjournments for an
  aggregate of more than ninety days, subsection 149(1) does not apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Business

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} All business transacted at a special meeting of
  shareholders and all business transacted at an annual meeting of
  shareholders, except consideration of the financial statements,
  auditor's report, election of directors and re-appointment of the
  incumbent auditor, is deemed to be special business.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of business

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Notice of a meeting of shareholders at which special
  business is to be transacted shall state

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nature of that business in sufficient detail to
    permit the shareholder to form a reasoned judgment thereon; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the text of any special resolution to be submitted
    to the meeting.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 135
- 2001, c. 14, s. 58
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Waiver of notice

**[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** A shareholder or any
other person entitled to attend a meeting of shareholders may in any
manner waive notice of a meeting of shareholders, and their attendance
at a meeting of shareholders is a waiver of notice of the meeting,
except where they attend a meeting for the express purpose of objecting
to the transaction of any business on the grounds that the meeting is
not lawfully called.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 136
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proposals

- **[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (1.1) and
  (1.2), a registered holder or beneficial owner of shares that are
  entitled to be voted at an annual meeting of shareholders may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} submit to the corporation notice of any matter that
    the person proposes to raise at the meeting (a "proposal"); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} discuss at the meeting any matter in respect of
    which the person would have been entitled to submit a proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons eligible to make proposals

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} To be eligible to submit a proposal, a person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} must be, for at least the prescribed period, the
    registered holder or the beneficial owner of at least the prescribed
    number of outstanding shares of the corporation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} must have the support of persons who, in the
    aggregate, and including or not including the person that submits
    the proposal, have been, for at least the prescribed period, the
    registered holders, or the beneficial owners of, at least the
    prescribed number of outstanding shares of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information to be provided

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} A proposal submitted under paragraph (1)(a) must be
  accompanied by the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of the person and of the
    person's supporters, if applicable; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number of shares held or owned by the person
    and the person's supporters, if applicable, and the date the shares
    were acquired.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information not part of proposal

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} The information provided under subsection (1.2)
  does not form part of the proposal or of the supporting statement
  referred to in subsection (3) and is not included for the purposes of
  the prescribed maximum word limit set out in subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof may be required

  [(1.4)]{.lawlabel} If requested by the corporation within the
  prescribed period, a person who submits a proposal must provide proof,
  within the prescribed period, that the person meets the requirements
  of subsection (1.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information circular

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A corporation that solicits proxies shall set out the
  proposal in the management proxy circular required by section 150 or
  attach the proposal thereto.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Supporting statement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If so requested by the person who submits a proposal,
  the corporation shall include in the management proxy circular or
  attach to it a statement in support of the proposal by the person and
  the name and address of the person. The statement and the proposal
  must together not exceed the prescribed maximum number of words.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Nomination for director

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A proposal may include nominations for the election
  of directors if the proposal is signed by one or more holders of
  shares representing in the aggregate not less than five per cent of
  the shares or five per cent of the shares of a class of shares of the
  corporation entitled to vote at the meeting to which the proposal is
  to be presented, but this subsection does not preclude nominations
  made at a meeting of shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemptions

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A corporation is not required to comply with
  subsections (2) and (3) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the proposal is not submitted to the corporation
    within the prescribed period;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it clearly appears that the primary purpose of the
    proposal is to enforce a personal claim or redress a personal
    grievance against the corporation or its directors, officers or
    security holders;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} it clearly appears that the proposal does not
    relate in a significant way to the business or affairs of the
    corporation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} not more than the prescribed period before the
    receipt of a proposal, a person failed to present, in person or by
    proxy, at a meeting of shareholders, a proposal that at the person's
    request, had been included in a management proxy circular relating
    to the meeting;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} substantially the same proposal was submitted to
    shareholders in a management proxy circular or a dissident's proxy
    circular relating to a meeting of shareholders held not more than
    the prescribed period before the receipt of the proposal and did not
    receive the prescribed minimum amount of support at the meeting; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the rights conferred by this section are being
    abused to secure publicity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corporation may refuse to include
  proposal

  [(5.1)]{.lawlabel} If a person who submits a proposal fails to
  continue to hold or own the number of shares referred to in subsection
  (1.1) up to and including the day of the meeting, the corporation is
  not required to set out in the management proxy circular, or attach to
  it, any proposal submitted by that person for any meeting held within
  the prescribed period following the date of the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No corporation or person acting on its behalf incurs
  any liability by reason only of circulating a proposal or statement in
  compliance with this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of refusal

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If a corporation refuses to include a proposal in a
  management proxy circular, the corporation shall, within the
  prescribed period after the day on which it receives the proposal or
  the day on which it receives the proof of ownership under subsection
  (1.4), as the case may be, notify in writing the person submitting the
  proposal of its intention to omit the proposal from the management
  proxy circular and of the reasons for the refusal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person may apply to court

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} On the application of a person submitting a proposal
  who claims to be aggrieved by a corporation's refusal under subsection
  (7), a court may restrain the holding of the meeting to which the
  proposal is sought to be presented and make any further order it
  thinks fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corporation's application to court

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The corporation or any person claiming to be
  aggrieved by a proposal may apply to a court for an order permitting
  the corporation to omit the proposal from the management proxy
  circular, and the court, if it is satisfied that subsection (5)
  applies, may make such order as it thinks fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Director entitled to notice

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} An applicant under subsection (8) or (9) shall give
  the Director notice of the application and the Director is entitled to
  appear and be heard in person or by counsel.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 137
- 2001, c. 14, s. 59
- 2011, c. 21, s. 53(F)
- [2018, c. 8, s. 15](#1037653-1373826){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1037653-1373826.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-137-20111129.html "Link to previous version of section 137"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List of shareholders entitled to receive
notice

- **[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  corporation shall prepare an alphabetical list of its shareholders
  entitled to receive notice of a meeting, showing the number of shares
  held by each shareholder,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if a record date is fixed under paragraph
    134(1)(c), not later than ten days after that date; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if no record date is fixed, on the record date
    established under paragraph 134(2)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voting list --- if record date fixed

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a record date for voting is fixed under paragraph
  134(1)(d), the corporation shall prepare, no later than ten days after
  the record date, an alphabetical list of shareholders entitled to vote
  as of the record date at a meeting of shareholders that shows the
  number of shares held by each shareholder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voting list --- if no record date fixed

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a record date for voting is not fixed under
  paragraph 134(1)(d), the corporation shall prepare, not later than 10
  days after the record date that is fixed under paragraph 134(1)(c) or
  not later than the record date that is established under paragraph
  134(2)(a), as the case may be, an alphabetical list of shareholders
  who are entitled to vote as of the record date that shows the number
  of shares held by each shareholder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entitlement to vote

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} A shareholder whose name appears on a list prepared
  under subsection (2) or (3) is entitled to vote the shares shown
  opposite their name at the meeting to which the list relates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination of list

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A shareholder may examine the list of shareholders

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} during usual business hours at the registered
    office of the corporation or at the place where its central
    securities register is maintained; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at the meeting of shareholders for which the list
    was prepared.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 138
- 2001, c. 14, s. 60
- 2018, c. 8, s. 16(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-138-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 138"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quorum

- **[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the by-laws otherwise
  provide, a quorum of shareholders is present at a meeting of
  shareholders, irrespective of the number of persons actually present
  at the meeting, if the holders of a majority of the shares entitled to
  vote at the meeting are present in person or represented by proxy.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Opening quorum sufficient

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a quorum is present at the opening of a meeting of
  shareholders, the shareholders present may, unless the by-laws
  otherwise provide, proceed with the business of the meeting,
  notwithstanding that a quorum is not present throughout the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjournment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a quorum is not present at the opening of a
  meeting of shareholders, the shareholders present may adjourn the
  meeting to a fixed time and place but may not transact any other
  business.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}One shareholder meeting

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a corporation has only one shareholder, or only
  one holder of any class or series of shares, the shareholder present
  in person or by proxy constitutes a meeting.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 133
- 1978-79, c. 9, ss. 1(F), 41
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to vote

- **[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the articles otherwise
  provide, each share of a corporation entitles the holder thereof to
  one vote at a meeting of shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representative

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a body corporate or association is a shareholder
  of a corporation, the corporation shall recognize any individual
  authorized by a resolution of the directors or governing body of the
  body corporate or association to represent it at meetings of
  shareholders of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of representative

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An individual authorized under subsection (2) may
  exercise on behalf of the body corporate or association all the powers
  it could exercise if it were an individual shareholder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Joint shareholders

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Unless the by-laws otherwise provide, if two or more
  persons hold shares jointly, one of those holders present at a meeting
  of shareholders may in the absence of the others vote the shares, but
  if two or more of those persons who are present, in person or by
  proxy, vote, they shall vote as one on the shares jointly held by
  them.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 140
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voting

- **[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the by-laws otherwise
  provide, voting at a meeting of shareholders shall be by show of hands
  except where a ballot is demanded by a shareholder or proxyholder
  entitled to vote at the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ballot

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A shareholder or proxyholder may demand a ballot
  either before or after any vote by show of hands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic voting

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), unless the by-laws otherwise
  provide, any vote referred to in subsection (1) may be held, in
  accordance with the regulations, if any, entirely by means of a
  telephonic, electronic or other communication facility, if the
  corporation makes available such a communication facility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voting while participating
  electronically

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Unless the by-laws otherwise provide, any person
  participating in a meeting of shareholders under subsection 132(4)
  or (5) and entitled to vote at that meeting may vote, in accordance
  with the regulations, if any, by means of the telephonic, electronic
  or other communication facility that the corporation has made
  available for that purpose.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 141
- 2001, c. 14, s. 61
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resolution in lieu of meeting

- **[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except where a written statement is
  submitted by a director under subsection 110(2) or by an auditor under
  subsection 168(5),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a resolution in writing signed by all the
    shareholders entitled to vote on that resolution at a meeting of
    shareholders is as valid as if it had been passed at a meeting of
    the shareholders; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a resolution in writing dealing with all matters
    required by this Act to be dealt with at a meeting of shareholders,
    and signed by all the shareholders entitled to vote at that meeting,
    satisfies all the requirements of this Act relating to meetings of
    shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing resolution

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A copy of every resolution referred to in
  subsection (1) shall be kept with the minutes of the meetings of
  shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless a ballot is demanded, an entry in the minutes
  of a meeting to the effect that the chairperson of the meeting
  declared a resolution to be carried or defeated is, in the absence of
  evidence to the contrary, proof of the fact without proof of the
  number or proportion of the votes recorded in favour of or against the
  resolution.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 142
- 2001, c. 14, s. 62
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requisition of meeting

- **[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  holders of not less than five per cent of the issued shares of a
  corporation that carry the right to vote at a meeting sought to be
  held may requisition the directors to call a meeting of shareholders
  for the purposes stated in the requisition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The requisition referred to in subsection (1), which
  may consist of several documents of like form each signed by one or
  more shareholders, shall state the business to be transacted at the
  meeting and shall be sent to each director and to the registered
  office of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directors calling meeting

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On receiving the requisition referred to in
  subsection (1), the directors shall call a meeting of shareholders to
  transact the business stated in the requisition, unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a record date has been fixed under paragraph
    134(1)(c) and notice of it has been given under subsection 134(3);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the directors have called a meeting of shareholders
    and have given notice thereof under section 135; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the business of the meeting as stated in the
    requisition includes matters described in paragraphs 137(5)(b) to
    (e).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shareholder calling meeting

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the directors do not within twenty-one days after
  receiving the requisition referred to in subsection (1) call a
  meeting, any shareholder who signed the requisition may call the
  meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A meeting called under this section shall be called
  as nearly as possible in the manner in which meetings are to be called
  pursuant to the by-laws, this Part and Part XIII.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reimbursement

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Unless the shareholders otherwise resolve at a
  meeting called under subsection (4), the corporation shall reimburse
  the shareholders the expenses reasonably incurred by them in
  requisitioning, calling and holding the meeting.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 143
- 2001, c. 14, s. 63
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meeting called by court

- **[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  court, on the application of a director, a shareholder who is entitled
  to vote at a meeting of shareholders or the Director, may order a
  meeting of a corporation to be called, held and conducted in the
  manner that the court directs, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is impracticable to call the meeting within the
    time or in the manner in which those meetings are to be called;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is impracticable to conduct the meeting in the
    manner required by this Act or the by-laws; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the court thinks that the meeting should be called,
    held and conducted within the time or in the manner it directs for
    any other reason.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Varying quorum

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Without restricting the generality of subsection (1),
  the court may order that the quorum required by the by-laws or this
  Act be varied or dispensed with at a meeting called, held and
  conducted pursuant to this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Valid meeting

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A meeting called, held and conducted pursuant to this
  section is for all purposes a meeting of shareholders of the
  corporation duly called, held and conducted.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 144
- 2001, c. 14, s. 64
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court review of election

- **[[145]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  corporation or a shareholder or director may apply to a court to
  determine any controversy with respect to an election or appointment
  of a director or auditor of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On an application under this section, the court may
  make any order it thinks fit including, without limiting the
  generality of the foregoing,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an order restraining a director or auditor whose
    election or appointment is challenged from acting pending
    determination of the dispute;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order declaring the result of the disputed
    election or appointment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an order requiring a new election or appointment,
    and including in the order directions for the management of the
    business and affairs of the corporation until a new election is held
    or appointment made; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an order determining the voting rights of
    shareholders and of persons claiming to own shares.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 145
- 2001, c. 14, s. 65(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pooling agreement

**[[145.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145.1 .sectionLabel}** A written agreement
between two or more shareholders may provide that in exercising voting
rights the shares held by them shall be voted as provided in the
agreement.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 14, s. 66
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unanimous shareholder agreement

- **[[146]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  otherwise lawful written agreement among all the shareholders of a
  corporation, or among all the shareholders and one or more persons who
  are not shareholders, that restricts, in whole or in part, the powers
  of the directors to manage, or supervise the management of, the
  business and affairs of the corporation is valid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration by single shareholder

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person who is the beneficial owner of all the
  issued shares of a corporation makes a written declaration that
  restricts in whole or in part the powers of the directors to manage,
  or supervise the management of, the business and affairs of the
  corporation, the declaration is deemed to be a unanimous shareholder
  agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Constructive party

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A purchaser or transferee of shares subject to a
  unanimous shareholder agreement is deemed to be a party to the
  agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When no notice given

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If notice is not given to a purchaser or transferee
  of the existence of a unanimous shareholder agreement, in the manner
  referred to in subsection 49(8) or otherwise, the purchaser or
  transferee may, no later than 30 days after they become aware of the
  existence of the unanimous shareholder agreement, rescind the
  transaction by which they acquired the shares.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights of shareholder

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} To the extent that a unanimous shareholder agreement
  restricts the powers of the directors to manage, or supervise the
  management of, the business and affairs of the corporation, parties to
  the unanimous shareholder agreement who are given that power to manage
  or supervise the management of the business and affairs of the
  corporation have all the rights, powers, duties and liabilities of a
  director of the corporation, whether they arise under this Act or
  otherwise, including any defences available to the directors, and the
  directors are relieved of their rights, powers, duties and
  liabilities, including their liabilities under section 119, to the
  same extent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discretion of shareholders

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section prevents shareholders from
  fettering their discretion when exercising the powers of directors
  under a unanimous shareholder agreement.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 146
- 1994, c. 24, s. 15(F)
- 2001, c. 14, s. 66
:::

## [PART XIII]{.HLabel1}[Proxies]{.HTitleText1} {#h-110393 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[147]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,

[[form of proxy]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[form of proxy]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a written or printed
    form that, on completion and execution or, in Quebec, on signing by
    or on behalf of a shareholder, becomes a proxy; ([formulaire de
    procuration]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[intermediary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[intermediary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who holds a
    security on behalf of another person who is not the registered
    holder of the security, and includes

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a securities broker or dealer required to be
      registered to trade or deal in securities under the laws of any
      jurisdiction;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a securities depositary;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a financial institution;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a clearing agency, a securities
      dealer, trust company, bank or other person, including another
      clearing agency, on whose behalf the clearing agency or its
      nominees hold securities of an issuer;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a trustee or administrator of a self-administered
      retirement savings plan, retirement income fund, education savings
      plan or other similar self-administered savings or investment plan
      registered under the [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3);

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a nominee of a person referred to in any of
      paragraphs (a) to (e); and

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a person who carries out functions similar to
      those carried out by individuals or entities referred to in any of
      paragraphs (a) to (e) and that holds a security registered in its
      name, or in the name of its nominee, on behalf of another person
      who is not the registered holder of the
      security. ([intermédiaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[proxy]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[proxy]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a completed and executed or, in
    Quebec, signed form of proxy by means of which a shareholder
    appoints a proxyholder to attend and act on the shareholder's behalf
    at a meeting of shareholders; ([procuration]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[registrant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[registrant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2001, c. 14, s.
    67\]]{.Repealed}

[[solicit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[solicit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} or
    [[solicitation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} includes

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a request for a proxy whether or not
        accompanied by or included in a form of proxy,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a request to execute or not to execute or, in
        Quebec, to sign or not to sign a form of proxy or to revoke a
        proxy,

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the sending of a form of proxy or other
        communication to a shareholder under circumstances reasonably
        calculated to result in the procurement, withholding or
        revocation of a proxy, and

      - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the sending of a form of proxy to a
        shareholder under section 149; but

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} does not include

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the sending of a form of proxy in response to
        an unsolicited request made by or on behalf of a shareholder,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the performance of administrative acts or
        professional services on behalf of a person soliciting a proxy,

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the sending by an intermediary of the
        documents referred to in section 153,

      - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a solicitation by a person in respect of
        shares of which the person is the beneficial owner,

      - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a public announcement, as prescribed, by a
        shareholder of how the shareholder intends to vote and the
        reasons for that decision,

      - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} a communication for the purposes of obtaining
        the number of shares required for a shareholder proposal under
        subsection 137(1.1), or

      - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} a communication, other than a solicitation by
        or on behalf of the management of the corporation, that is made
        to shareholders, in any circumstances that may be
        prescribed; ([sollicitation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[solicitation by or on behalf of the management of a corporation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[solicitation by or on behalf of the management of a
    corporation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a solicitation by any person
    pursuant to a resolution or instructions of, or with the
    acquiescence of, the directors or a committee of the
    directors. ([sollicitation effectuée par la direction ou pour son
    compte]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 147
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 67, 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 54(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-147-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 147"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointing proxyholder

- **[[148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  shareholder entitled to vote at a meeting of shareholders may by means
  of a proxy appoint a proxyholder or one or more alternate proxyholders
  who are not required to be shareholders, to attend and act at the
  meeting in the manner and to the extent authorized by the proxy and
  with the authority conferred by the proxy.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Execution or signing of proxy

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A proxy shall be executed or, in Quebec, signed by
  the shareholder or by the shareholder's personal representative
  authorized in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity of proxy

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A proxy is valid only at the meeting in respect of
  which it is given or any adjournment thereof.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation of proxy

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A shareholder may revoke a proxy

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by depositing an instrument or act in writing
    executed or, in Quebec, signed by the shareholder or by the
    shareholder's personal representative authorized in writing

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at the registered office of the corporation at
      any time up to and including the last business day preceding the
      day of the meeting, or an adjournment thereof, at which the proxy
      is to be used, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} with the chairman of the meeting on the day of
      the meeting or an adjournment thereof; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other manner permitted by law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deposit of proxies

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The directors may specify in a notice calling a
  meeting of shareholders a time not more than 48 hours, excluding
  Saturdays and holidays, before the meeting or adjournment before which
  time proxies to be used at the meeting must be deposited with the
  corporation or its agent or mandatary.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 148
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 55
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-148-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 148"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandatory solicitation

- **[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  management of a corporation shall, concurrently with giving notice of
  a meeting of shareholders, send a form of proxy in prescribed form to
  each shareholder who is entitled to receive notice of the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The management of the corporation is not required to
  send a form of proxy under subsection (1) if it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is not a distributing corporation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has fifty or fewer shareholders entitled to vote at
    a meeting, two or more joint holders being counted as one
    shareholder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the management of a corporation fails to comply,
  without reasonable cause, with subsection (1), the corporation is
  guilty of an offence and liable on summary conviction to a fine not
  exceeding five thousand dollars.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officers, etc., of corporations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a corporation commits an offence under
  subsection (3), any director or officer of the corporation who
  knowingly authorized, permitted or acquiesced in the commission of the
  offence is a party to and guilty of the offence and is liable on
  summary conviction to a fine not exceeding five thousand dollars or to
  imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months or to both, whether
  or not the corporation has been prosecuted or convicted.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 149
- 2001, c. 14, s. 68
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Soliciting proxies

- **[[150]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person shall not solicit proxies unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of solicitation by or on behalf of the
    management of a corporation, a management proxy circular in
    prescribed form, either as an appendix to or as a separate document
    accompanying the notice of the meeting, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any other solicitation, a
    dissident's proxy circular in prescribed form stating the purposes
    of the solicitation

  is sent to the auditor of the corporation, to each shareholder whose
  proxy is solicited, to each director and, if paragraph (b) applies, to
  the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- solicitation to fifteen
  or fewer shareholders

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a person may solicit
  proxies, other than by or on behalf of the management of the
  corporation, without sending a dissident's proxy circular, if the
  total number of shareholders whose proxies are solicited is fifteen or
  fewer, two or more joint holders being counted as one shareholder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- solicitation by public
  broadcast

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a person may solicit
  proxies, other than by or on behalf of the management of the
  corporation, without sending a dissident's proxy circular if the
  solicitation is, in the prescribed circumstances, conveyed by public
  broadcast, speech or publication.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to Director

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person required to send a management proxy circular
  or dissident's proxy circular shall send concurrently a copy of it to
  the Director together with a statement in prescribed form, the form of
  proxy, any other documents for use in connection with the meeting and,
  in the case of a management proxy circular, a copy of the notice of
  meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who fails to comply with subsections (1)
  and (2) is guilty of an offence and liable on summary conviction to a
  fine not exceeding five thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term
  not exceeding six months or to both, whether or not the body corporate
  has been prosecuted or convicted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officers, etc., of bodies corporate

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a body corporate commits an offence under
  subsection (3), any director or officer of the body corporate who
  knowingly authorized, permitted or acquiesced in the commission of the
  offence is a party to and guilty of the offence and is liable on
  summary conviction to a fine not exceeding five thousand dollars or to
  imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months or to both, whether
  or not the body corporate has been prosecuted or convicted.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 150
- 1992, c. 1, s. 54
- 1994, c. 24, s. 16
- 2001, c. 14, s. 69
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

- **[[151]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  the application of an interested person, the Director may exempt the
  person, on any terms that the Director thinks fit, from any of the
  requirements of section 149 or subsection 150(1) or 153(1). The
  exemption may have retroactive effect.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 8, s. 18\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 151
- 2001, c. 14, s. 70
- 2018, c. 8, s. 18
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-151-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 151"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attendance at meeting

- **[[152]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who solicits a proxy and is appointed proxyholder shall attend
  in person or cause an alternate proxyholder to attend the meeting in
  respect of which the proxy is given and comply with the directions of
  the shareholder who appointed him.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of a proxyholder

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A proxyholder or an alternate proxyholder has the
  same rights as the shareholder by whom they were appointed to speak at
  a meeting of shareholders in respect of any matter, to vote by way of
  ballot at the meeting and, except where a proxyholder or an alternate
  proxyholder has conflicting instructions from more than one
  shareholder, to vote at such a meeting in respect of any matter by way
  of any show of hands.
