- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Show of hands

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (1) and (2), if the chairperson
  of a meeting of shareholders declares to the meeting that, if a ballot
  is conducted, the total number of votes attached to shares represented
  at the meeting by proxy required to be voted against what to the
  knowledge of the chairperson will be the decision of the meeting in
  relation to any matter or group of matters is less than five per cent
  of all the votes that might be cast by shareholders personally or
  through proxy at the meeting on the ballot, unless a shareholder or
  proxyholder demands a ballot,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the chairperson may conduct the vote in respect of
    that matter or group of matters by a show of hands; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a proxyholder or alternate proxyholder may vote in
    respect of that matter or group of matters by a show of hands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A proxyholder or alternate proxyholder who without
  reasonable cause fails to comply with the directions of a shareholder
  under this section is guilty of an offence and liable on summary
  conviction to a fine not exceeding five thousand dollars or to
  imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 152
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 71, 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of intermediary

- **[[153]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-153
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Shares of a corporation that are
  registered in the name of an intermediary or their nominee and not
  beneficially owned by the intermediary must not be voted unless the
  intermediary, without delay after receipt of the notice of the
  meeting, financial statements, management proxy circular, dissident's
  proxy circular and any other documents other than the form of proxy
  sent to shareholders by or on behalf of any person for use in
  connection with the meeting, sends a copy of the document to the
  beneficial owner and, except when the intermediary has received
  written voting instructions from the beneficial owner, a written
  request for such instructions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction on voting

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An intermediary, or a proxyholder appointed by an
  intermediary, may not vote shares that the intermediary does not
  beneficially own and that are registered in the name of the
  intermediary or in the name of a nominee of the intermediary unless
  the intermediary or proxyholder, as the case may be, receives written
  voting instructions from the beneficial owner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person by or on behalf of whom a solicitation is
  made shall provide, at the request of an intermediary, without delay,
  to the intermediary at the person's expense the necessary number of
  copies of the documents referred to in subsection (1), other than
  copies of the document requesting voting instructions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Instructions to intermediary

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An intermediary shall vote or appoint a proxyholder
  to vote any shares referred to in subsection (1) in accordance with
  any written voting instructions received from the beneficial owner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Beneficial owner as proxyholder

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a beneficial owner so requests and provides an
  intermediary with appropriate documentation, the intermediary must
  appoint the beneficial owner or a nominee of the beneficial owner as
  proxyholder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The failure of an intermediary to comply with this
  section does not render void any meeting of shareholders or any action
  taken at the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section gives an intermediary the
  right to vote shares that the intermediary is otherwise prohibited
  from voting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} An intermediary who knowingly fails to comply with
  this section is guilty of an offence and liable on summary conviction
  to a fine not exceeding five thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a
  term not exceeding six months or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officers, etc., of bodies corporate

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If an intermediary that is a body corporate commits
  an offence under subsection (8), any director or officer of the body
  corporate who knowingly authorized, permitted or acquiesced in the
  commission of the offence is a party to and guilty of the offence and
  is liable on summary conviction to a fine not exceeding five thousand
  dollars or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months or to
  both, whether or not the body corporate has been prosecuted or
  convicted.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 153
- 2001, c. 14, s. 72
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restraining order

- **[[154]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-154 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  form of proxy, management proxy circular or dissident's proxy circular
  contains an untrue statement of a material fact or omits to state a
  material fact required therein or necessary to make a statement
  contained therein not misleading in the light of the circumstances in
  which it was made, an interested person or the Director may apply to a
  court and the court may make any order it thinks fit including,
  without limiting the generality of the foregoing,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an order restraining the solicitation, the holding
    of the meeting, or any person from implementing or acting on any
    resolution passed at the meeting to which the form of proxy,
    management proxy circular or dissident's proxy circular relates;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order requiring correction of any form of proxy
    or proxy circular and a further solicitation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an order adjourning the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Director

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An applicant under this section shall give to the
  Director notice of the application and the Director is entitled to
  appear and to be heard in person or by counsel.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 148
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

## [PART XIV]{.HLabel1}[Financial Disclosure]{.HTitleText1} {#h-110522 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual financial statements

- **[[155]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  directors of a corporation shall place before the shareholders at
  every annual meeting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribed comparative financial statements that
    conform to any prescribed requirements and relate separately to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the period that began on the date the corporation
      came into existence and ended not more than six months before the
      annual meeting or, if the corporation has completed a financial
      year, the period that began immediately after the end of the last
      completed financial year and ended not more than six months before
      the annual meeting, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the immediately preceding financial year;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the report of the auditor, if any; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any further information respecting the financial
    position of the corporation and the results of its operations
    required by the articles, the by-laws or any unanimous shareholder
    agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding paragraph (1)(a), the financial
  statements referred to in subparagraph (1)(a)(ii) may be omitted if
  the reason for the omission is set out in the financial statements, or
  in a note thereto, to be placed before the shareholders at an annual
  meeting.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 155
- 2018, c. 8, s. 20
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-155-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 155"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for exemption

**[[156]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-156 .sectionLabel}** On the application of a
corporation, the Director may exempt the corporation, on any terms that
the Director thinks fit, from any requirement set out in section 155 or
any of sections 157 to 160, if the Director reasonably believes that the
detriment that may be caused to the corporation by the requirement
outweighs its benefit to the shareholders or, in the case of a
distributing corporation, to the public.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 156
- 2001, c. 14, s. 74
- 2018, c. 8, s. 21
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-156-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 156"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consolidated statements

- **[[157]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  corporation shall keep at its registered office a copy of the
  financial statements of each of its subsidiary bodies corporate and of
  each body corporate the accounts of which are consolidated in the
  financial statements of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Shareholders of a corporation and their personal
  representatives may on request examine the statements referred to in
  subsection (1) during the usual business hours of the corporation and
  may make extracts free of charge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Barring examination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A corporation may, within fifteen days of a request
  to examine under subsection (2), apply to a court for an order barring
  the right of any person to so examine, and the court may, if it is
  satisfied that such examination would be detrimental to the
  corporation or a subsidiary body corporate, bar such right and make
  any further order it thinks fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Director

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A corporation shall give the Director and the person
  asking to examine under subsection (2) notice of an application under
  subsection (3), and the Director and such person may appear and be
  heard in person or by counsel.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 157
- 2001, c. 14, s. 75
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of financial statements

- **[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  directors of a corporation shall approve the financial statements
  referred to in section 155 and the approval shall be evidenced by the
  manual signature of one or more directors or a facsimile of the
  signatures reproduced in the statements.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Condition precedent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A corporation shall not issue, publish or circulate
  copies of the financial statements referred to in section 155 unless
  the financial statements are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} approved and signed in accordance with subsection
    (1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} accompanied by the report of the auditor of the
    corporation, if any.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 158
- 2001, c. 14, s. 76
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies to shareholders

- **[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  corporation shall, not less than twenty-one days before each annual
  meeting of shareholders or before the signing of a resolution under
  paragraph 142(1)(b) in lieu of the annual meeting, send a copy of the
  documents referred to in section 155 to each shareholder, except to a
  shareholder who has informed the corporation in writing that he or she
  does not want a copy of those documents.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A corporation that, without reasonable cause, fails
  to comply with subsection (1) is guilty of an offence and liable on
  summary conviction to a fine not exceeding five thousand dollars.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 159
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies to Director

- **[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  distributing corporation, any of the issued securities of which remain
  outstanding and are held by more than one person, shall send a copy of
  the documents referred to in section 155 to the Director

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} not less than twenty-one days before each annual
    meeting of shareholders, or without delay after a resolution
    referred to in paragraph 142(1)(b) is signed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any event within fifteen months after the last
    preceding annual meeting should have been held or a resolution in
    lieu of the meeting should have been signed, but no later than six
    months after the end of the corporation's preceding financial year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsidiary corporation exemption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A subsidiary corporation is not required to comply
  with this section if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the financial statements of its holding corporation
    are in consolidated or combined form and include the accounts of the
    subsidiary; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the consolidated or combined financial statements
    of the holding corporation are included in the documents sent to the
    Director by the holding corporation in compliance with this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A corporation that fails to comply with this section
  is guilty of an offence and is liable on summary conviction to a fine
  not exceeding five thousand dollars.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 160
- 1992, c. 1, s. 55
- 1994, c. 24, s. 17
- 2001, c. 14, s. 77
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualification of auditor

- **[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (5), a person
  is disqualified from being an auditor of a corporation if the person
  is not independent of the corporation, any of its affiliates, or the
  directors or officers of any such corporation or its affiliates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Independence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} independence is a question of fact; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person is deemed not to be independent if they or
    their business partner

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is a business partner, a director, an officer or
      an employee of the corporation or any of its affiliates, or a
      business partner of any director, officer or employee of any such
      corporation or any of its affiliates,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} beneficially owns or controls, directly or
      indirectly, a material interest in the securities of the
      corporation or any of its affiliates, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} has been a receiver, receiver-manager,
      sequestrator, liquidator or trustee in bankruptcy of the
      corporation or any of its affiliates within two years of the
      person's proposed appointment as auditor of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Business partners

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (2), a person's
  business partner includes a shareholder of that person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to resign

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An auditor who becomes disqualified under this
  section shall, subject to subsection (5), resign forthwith after
  becoming aware of the disqualification.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disqualification order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An interested person may apply to a court for an
  order declaring an auditor to be disqualified under this section and
  the office of auditor to be vacant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption order

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An interested person may apply to a court for an
  order exempting an auditor from disqualification under this section
  and the court may, if it is satisfied that an exemption would not
  unfairly prejudice the shareholders, make an exemption order on such
  terms as it thinks fit, which order may have retrospective effect.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 161
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 78, 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 56(E)
- 2018, c. 8, s. 23(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-161-20111129.html "Link to previous version of section 161"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of auditor

- **[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 163, shareholders
  of a corporation shall, by ordinary resolution, at the first annual
  meeting of shareholders and at each succeeding annual meeting, appoint
  an auditor to hold office until the close of the next annual meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligibility

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An auditor appointed under section 104 is eligible
  for appointment under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incumbent auditor

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (1), if an auditor is not
  appointed at a meeting of shareholders, the incumbent auditor
  continues in office until a successor is appointed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The remuneration of an auditor may be fixed by
  ordinary resolution of the shareholders or, if not so fixed, may be
  fixed by the directors.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 156
- 1978-79, c. 9, ss. 1(F), 48
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dispensing with auditor

- **[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  shareholders of a corporation that is not a distributing corporation
  may resolve not to appoint an auditor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A resolution under subsection (1) is valid only until
  the next succeeding annual meeting of shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unanimous consent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A resolution under subsection (1) is not valid unless
  it is consented to by all the shareholders, including shareholders not
  otherwise entitled to vote.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1994, c. 24, s. 18\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 163
- 1992, c. 1, s. 56
- 1994, c. 24, s. 18
- 2001, c. 14, s. 79
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ceasing to hold office

- **[[164]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  auditor of a corporation ceases to hold office when the auditor

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} dies or resigns; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is removed pursuant to section 165.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date of resignation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A resignation of an auditor becomes effective at the
  time a written resignation is sent to the corporation, or at the time
  specified in the resignation, whichever is later.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 164
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of auditor

- **[[165]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  shareholders of a corporation may by ordinary resolution at a special
  meeting remove from office the auditor other than an auditor appointed
  by a court under section 167.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vacancy

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A vacancy created by the removal of an auditor may be
  filled at the meeting at which the auditor is removed or, if not so
  filled, may be filled under section 166.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 159
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filling vacancy

- **[[166]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), the
  directors shall forthwith fill a vacancy in the office of auditor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calling meeting

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If there is not a quorum of directors, the directors
  then in office shall, within twenty-one days after a vacancy in the
  office of auditor occurs, call a special meeting of shareholders to
  fill the vacancy and, if they fail to call a meeting or if there are
  no directors, the meeting may be called by any shareholder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shareholders filling vacancy

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The articles of a corporation may provide that a
  vacancy in the office of auditor shall only be filled by vote of the
  shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unexpired term

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An auditor appointed to fill a vacancy holds office
  for the unexpired term of the auditor's predecessor.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 166
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court appointed auditor

- **[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  corporation does not have an auditor, the court may, on the
  application of a shareholder or the Director, appoint and fix the
  remuneration of an auditor who holds office until an auditor is
  appointed by the shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if the shareholders
  have resolved under section 163 not to appoint an auditor.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 161
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to attend meeting

- **[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  auditor of a corporation is entitled to receive notice of every
  meeting of shareholders and, at the expense of the corporation, to
  attend and be heard on matters relating to the auditor's duties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to attend

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a director or shareholder of a corporation,
  whether or not the shareholder is entitled to vote at the meeting,
  gives written notice not less than ten days before a meeting of
  shareholders to the auditor or a former auditor of the corporation,
  the auditor or former auditor shall attend the meeting at the expense
  of the corporation and answer questions relating to their duties as
  auditor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to corporation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A director or shareholder who sends a notice referred
  to in subsection (2) shall send concurrently a copy of the notice to
  the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An auditor or former auditor of a corporation who
  fails without reasonable cause to comply with subsection (2) is guilty
  of an offence and liable on summary conviction to a fine not exceeding
  five thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding six
  months or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement of auditor

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An auditor is entitled to submit to the corporation a
  written statement giving reasons for resigning or for opposing any
  proposed action or resolution when the auditor

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} resigns;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} receives a notice or otherwise learns of a meeting
    of shareholders called for the purpose of removing the auditor from
    office;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} receives a notice or otherwise learns of a meeting
    of directors or shareholders at which another person is to be
    appointed to fill the office of auditor, whether because of the
    resignation or removal of the incumbent auditor or because the
    auditor's term of office has expired or is about to expire; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} receives a notice or otherwise learns of a meeting
    of shareholders at which a resolution referred to in section 163 is
    to be proposed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other statements

  [(5.1)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a proposed replacement of an
  auditor, whether through removal or at the end of the auditor's term,
  the following rules apply with respect to other statements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the corporation shall make a statement on the
    reasons for the proposed replacement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the proposed replacement auditor may make a
    statement in which he or she comments on the reasons referred to in
    paragraph (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Circulating statement

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The corporation shall send a copy of the statements
  referred to in subsections (5) and (5.1) without delay to every
  shareholder entitled to receive notice of a meeting referred to in
  subsection (1) and to the Director, unless the statement is included
  in or attached to a management proxy circular required by section 150.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Replacing auditor

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} No person shall accept appointment or consent to be
  appointed as auditor of a corporation to replace an auditor who has
  resigned, been removed or whose term of office has expired or is about
  to expire until the person has requested and received from that
  auditor a written statement of the circumstances and the reasons, in
  that auditor's opinion, for their replacement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (7), a person otherwise
  qualified may accept appointment or consent to be appointed as auditor
  of a corporation if, within fifteen days after making the request
  referred to in that subsection, the person does not receive a reply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of non-compliance

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Unless subsection (8) applies, an appointment as
  auditor of a corporation of a person who has not complied with
  subsection (7) is void.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 168
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 80, 135(E)
- 2018, c. 8, s. 23.1(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-168-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 168"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination

- **[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  auditor of a corporation shall make the examination that is in their
  opinion necessary to enable them to report in the prescribed manner on
  the financial statements required by this Act to be placed before the
  shareholders, except such financial statements or part thereof that
  relate to the period referred to in subparagraph 155(1)(a)(ii).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reliance on other auditor

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding section 170, an auditor of a
  corporation may reasonably rely on the report of an auditor of a body
  corporate or an unincorporated business the accounts of which are
  included in whole or in part in the financial statements of the
  corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasonableness

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (2), reasonableness is
  a question of fact.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) applies whether or not the financial
  statements of the holding corporation reported on by the auditor are
  in consolidated form.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 169
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to information

- **[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  the demand of an auditor of a corporation, the present or former
  directors, officers, employees, agents or mandataries of the
  corporation shall provide any

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} information and explanations, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} access to records, documents, books, accounts and
    vouchers of the corporation or any of its subsidiaries

  that are, in the opinion of the auditor, necessary to enable the
  auditor to make the examination and report required under section 169
  and that the directors, officers, employees, agents or mandataries are
  reasonably able to provide.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On the demand of the auditor of a corporation, the
  directors of the corporation shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} obtain from the present or former directors,
    officers, employees and agents or mandataries of any subsidiary of
    the corporation the information and explanations that the present or
    former directors, officers, employees and agents or mandataries are
    reasonably able to provide and that are, in the opinion of the
    auditor, necessary to enable the auditor to make the examination and
    report required under section 169; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} furnish the auditor with the information and
    explanations so obtained.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No civil liability

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who in good faith makes an oral or written
  communication under subsection (1) or (2) is not liable in any civil
  proceeding arising from having made the communication.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 170
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 81, 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 57(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-170-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 170"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Audit committee

- **[[171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a
  corporation described in subsection 102(2) shall, and any other
  corporation may, have an audit committee composed of not less than
  three directors of the corporation, a majority of whom are not
  officers or employees of the corporation or any of its affiliates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Director may, on the application of a
  corporation, authorize the corporation to dispense with an audit
  committee, and the Director may, if satisfied that the shareholders
  will not be prejudiced, permit the corporation to dispense with an
  audit committee on any reasonable conditions that the Director thinks
  fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of committee

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An audit committee shall review the financial
  statements of the corporation before such financial statements are
  approved under section 158.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Auditor's attendance

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The auditor of a corporation is entitled to receive
  notice of every meeting of the audit committee and, at the expense of
  the corporation, to attend and be heard thereat; and, if so requested
  by a member of the audit committee, shall attend every meeting of the
  committee held during the term of office of the auditor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calling meeting

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The auditor of a corporation or a member of the audit
  committee may call a meeting of the committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of errors

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A director or an officer of a corporation shall
  forthwith notify the audit committee and the auditor of any error or
  mis-statement of which the director or officer becomes aware in a
  financial statement that the auditor or a former auditor has reported
  on.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Error in financial statements

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An auditor or former auditor of a corporation who is
  notified or becomes aware of an error or mis-statement in a financial
  statement on which they have reported, if in their opinion the error
  or mis-statement is material, shall inform each director accordingly.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of directors

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} When under subsection (7) the auditor or former
  auditor informs the directors of an error or mis-statement in a
  financial statement, the directors shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prepare and issue revised financial statements; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} otherwise inform the shareholders and, if the
    corporation is one that is required to comply with section 160, it
    shall inform the Director of the error or mis-statement in the same
    manner as it informs the shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Every director or officer of a corporation who
  knowingly fails to comply with subsection (6) or (8) is guilty of an
  offence and liable on summary conviction to a fine not exceeding five
  thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding six
  months or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 171
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 82, 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualified privilege (defamation)

**[[172]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172 .sectionLabel}** Any oral or written
statement or report made under this Act by the auditor or former auditor
of a corporation has qualified privilege.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 166
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

## [PART XIV.1]{.HLabel1}[Disclosure Relating to Diversity]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1215060 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Diversity in corporations

- **[[172.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The directors of a prescribed
  corporation shall place before the shareholders, at every annual
  meeting, the prescribed information respecting diversity among the
  directors and among the [[members of senior
  management]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information to shareholders and
  Director

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The corporation shall provide the information
  referred to in subsection (1) to each shareholder, except to a
  shareholder who has informed the corporation in writing that they do
  not want to receive that information, by sending the information along
  with the notice referred to in subsection 135(1) or by making the
  information available along with a proxy circular referred to in
  subsection 150(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information to Director

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The corporation shall concurrently send the
  information referred to in subsection (1) to the Director.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 8, s. 24](#1037669-1215069){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1037669-1215069.html"
  role="button"}
:::

## [PART XV]{.HLabel1}[Fundamental Changes]{.HTitleText1} {#h-110735 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment of articles

- **[[173]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 176 and 177, the
  articles of a corporation may by special resolution be amended to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} change its name;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} change the province in which its registered office
    is situated;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} add, change or remove any restriction on the
    business or businesses that the corporation may carry on;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} change any maximum number of shares that the
    corporation is authorized to issue;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} create new classes of shares;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} reduce or increase its stated capital, if its
    stated capital is set out in the articles;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} change the designation of all or any of its shares,
    and add, change or remove any rights, privileges, restrictions and
    conditions, including rights to accrued dividends, in respect of all
    or any of its shares, whether issued or unissued;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} change the shares of any class or series, whether
    issued or unissued, into a different number of shares of the same
    class or series or into the same or a different number of shares of
    other classes or series;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} divide a class of shares, whether issued or
    unissued, into series and fix the number of shares in each series
    and the rights, privileges, restrictions and conditions thereof;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} authorize the directors to divide any class of
    unissued shares into series and fix the number of shares in each
    series and the rights, privileges, restrictions and conditions
    thereof;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} authorize the directors to change the rights,
    privileges, restrictions and conditions attached to unissued shares
    of any series;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} revoke, diminish or enlarge any authority conferred
    under paragraphs (j) and (k);

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} increase or decrease the number of directors or the
    minimum or maximum number of directors, subject to sections 107 and
    112;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} add, change or remove restrictions on the issue,
    transfer or ownership of shares; or

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} add, change or remove any other provision that is
    permitted by this Act to be set out in the articles.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The directors of a corporation may, if authorized by
  the shareholders in the special resolution effecting an amendment
  under this section, revoke the resolution before it is acted on
  without further approval of the shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment of number name

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (1), where a corporation
  has a designating number as a name, the directors may amend its
  articles to change that name to a verbal name.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 173
- 1994, c. 24, s. 19
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 83, 134(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Constraints on shares

- **[[174]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 176 and 177, a
  distributing corporation, any of the issued shares of which remain
  outstanding and are held by more than one person, may by special
  resolution amend its articles in accordance with the regulations to
  constrain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the issue or transfer of shares of any class or
    series to persons who are not resident Canadians;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the issue or transfer of shares of any class or
    series to enable the corporation or any of its affiliates or
    associates to qualify under any prescribed law of Canada or a
    province

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to obtain a licence to carry on any business,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to become a publisher of a Canadian newspaper or
      periodical, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} to acquire shares of a financial intermediary
      as defined in the regulations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the issue, transfer or ownership of shares of any
    class or series in order to assist the corporation or any of its
    affiliates or associates to qualify under any prescribed law of
    Canada or a province to receive licences, permits, grants, payments
    or other benefits by reason of attaining or maintaining a specified
    level of Canadian ownership or control;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the issue, transfer or ownership of shares of any
    class or series in order to assist the corporation to comply with
    any prescribed law.

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the issue, transfer or ownership of shares of any
    class or series to enable the corporation to be a registered
    labour-sponsored venture capital corporation under Part X.3 of the
    [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception in respect of paragraph
  (1)(c)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(c) does not permit a constraint on the
  issue, transfer or ownership of shares of any class or series of which
  any shares are outstanding unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a constraint in respect of a class,
    the shares of the class, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a constraint in respect of a series,
    the shares of the series

  are already subject to a constraint permitted under that paragraph.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on ownership of shares

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A corporation may, pursuant to paragraph (1)(c),
  limit the number of shares of that corporation that may be owned, or
  prohibit the ownership of shares, by any person whose ownership would
  adversely affect the ability of the corporation or any of its
  affiliates or associates to attain or maintain a level of Canadian
  ownership or control specified in its articles that equals or exceeds
  a specified level referred to in paragraph (1)(c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change or removal of constraint

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A corporation referred to in subsection (1) may by
  special resolution amend its articles to change or remove any
  constraint on the issue, transfer or ownership of its shares.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The directors of a corporation may, if authorized by
  the shareholders in the special resolution effecting an amendment
  under subsection (1) or (4), revoke the resolution before it is acted
  on without further approval of the shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections 261(2) and (3), the Governor
  in Council may make regulations with respect to a corporation that
  constrains the issue, transfer or ownership of its shares prescribing

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the disclosure required of the constraints in
    documents issued or published by the corporation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the duties and powers of the directors to refuse to
    issue or register transfers of shares in accordance with the
    articles of the corporation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the limitations on voting rights of any shares held
    contrary to the articles of the corporation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the powers of the directors to require disclosure
    of beneficial ownership of shares of the corporation and the right
    of the corporation and its directors, employees and agents or
    mandataries to rely on that disclosure and the effects of that
    reliance; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the rights of any person owning shares of the
    corporation at the time of an amendment to its articles constraining
    share issues or transfers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity of acts

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An issue or a transfer of a share or an act of a
  corporation is valid notwithstanding any failure to comply with this
  section or the regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 174
- 1991, c. 45, s. 554, c. 47, s. 722
- 1994, c. 21, s. 125
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 84, 134(F)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 58(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-174-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 174"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proposal to amend

- **[[175]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-175
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a
  director or a shareholder who is entitled to vote at an annual meeting
  of shareholders may, in accordance with section 137, make a proposal
  to amend the articles.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of amendment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notice of a meeting of shareholders at which a
  proposal to amend the articles is to be considered shall set out the
  proposed amendment and, where applicable, shall state that a
  dissenting shareholder is entitled to be paid the fair value of their
  shares in accordance with section 190, but failure to make that
  statement does not invalidate an amendment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 175
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class vote

- **[[176]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-176 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  holders of shares of a class or, subject to subsection (4), of a
  series are, unless the articles otherwise provide in the case of an
  amendment referred to in paragraphs (a), (b) and (e), entitled to vote
  separately as a class or series on a proposal to amend the articles to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} increase or decrease any maximum number of
    authorized shares of such class, or increase any maximum number of
    authorized shares of a class having rights or privileges equal or
    superior to the shares of such class;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} effect an exchange, reclassification or
    cancellation of all or part of the shares of such class;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} add, change or remove the rights, privileges,
    restrictions or conditions attached to the shares of such class and,
    without limiting the generality of the foregoing,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} remove or change prejudicially rights to accrued
      dividends or rights to cumulative dividends,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} add, remove or change prejudicially redemption
      rights,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} reduce or remove a dividend preference or a
      liquidation preference, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} add, remove or change prejudicially conversion
      privileges, options, voting, transfer or pre-emptive rights, or
      rights to acquire securities of a corporation, or sinking fund
      provisions;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} increase the rights or privileges of any class of
    shares having rights or privileges equal or superior to the shares
    of such class;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} create a new class of shares equal or superior to
    the shares of such class;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} make any class of shares having rights or
    privileges inferior to the shares of such class equal or superior to
    the shares of such class;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} effect an exchange or create a right of exchange of
    all or part of the shares of another class into the shares of such
    class; or

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} constrain the issue, transfer or ownership of the
    shares of such class or change or remove such constraint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of a
  proposal to amend the articles to add a right or privilege for a
  holder to convert shares of a class or series into shares of another
  class or series that is subject to a constraint permitted under
  paragraph 174(1)(c) but is otherwise equal to the class or series
  first mentioned.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deeming provision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (1)(e), a new class of
  shares, the issue, transfer or ownership of which is to be constrained
  by an amendment to the articles pursuant to paragraph 174(1)(c), that
  is otherwise equal to an existing class of shares shall be deemed not
  to be equal or superior to the existing class of shares.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The holders of a series of shares of a class are
  entitled to vote separately as a series under subsection (1) only if
  such series is affected by an amendment in a manner different from
  other shares of the same class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to vote

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies whether or not shares of a
  class or series otherwise carry the right to vote.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Separate resolutions

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A proposed amendment to the articles referred to in
  subsection (1) is adopted when the holders of the shares of each class
  or series entitled to vote separately thereon as a class or series
  have approved such amendment by a special resolution.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 176
- 2001, c. 14, s. 134(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivery of articles

- **[[177]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-177
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any revocation under
  subsection 173(2) or 174(5), after an amendment has been adopted under
  section 173, 174 or 176 articles of amendment in the form that the
  Director fixes shall be sent to the Director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reduction of stated capital

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an amendment effects or requires a reduction of
  stated capital, subsections 38(3) and (4) apply.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 177
- 2001, c. 14, s. 85
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of amendment

**[[178]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178 .sectionLabel}** On receipt of articles
of amendment, the Director shall issue a certificate of amendment in
accordance with section 262.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 172
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

- **[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  amendment becomes effective on the date shown in the certificate of
  amendment and the articles are amended accordingly.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights preserved

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No amendment to the articles affects an existing
  cause of action or claim or liability to prosecution in favour of or
  against the corporation or its directors or officers, or any civil,
  criminal or administrative action or proceeding to which a corporation
  or its directors or officers is a party.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 173
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restated articles

- **[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  directors may at any time, and shall when reasonably so directed by
  the Director, restate the articles of incorporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivery of articles

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Restated articles of incorporation in the form that
  the Director fixes shall be sent to the Director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restated certificate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of restated articles of incorporation, the
  Director shall issue a restated certificate of incorporation in
  accordance with section 262.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Restated articles of incorporation are effective on
  the date shown in the restated certificate of incorporation and
  supersede the original articles of incorporation and all amendments
  thereto.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 180
- 2001, c. 14, s. 86
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amalgamation

**[[181]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181 .sectionLabel}** Two or more
corporations, including holding and subsidiary corporations, may
amalgamate and continue as one corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 175
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amalgamation agreement

- **[[182]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each
  corporation proposing to amalgamate shall enter into an agreement
  setting out the terms and means of effecting the amalgamation and, in
  particular, setting out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the provisions that are required to be included in
    articles of incorporation under section 6;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of each proposed director of
    the amalgamated corporation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which the shares of each amalgamating
    corporation are to be converted into shares or other securities of
    the amalgamated corporation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if any shares of an amalgamating corporation are
    not to be converted into securities of the amalgamated corporation,
    the amount of money or securities of any body corporate that the
    holders of such shares are to receive in addition to or instead of
    securities of the amalgamated corporation;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the manner of payment of money instead of the issue
    of fractional shares of the amalgamated corporation or of any other
    body corporate the securities of which are to be received in the
    amalgamation;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} whether the by-laws of the amalgamated corporation
    are to be those of one of the amalgamating corporations and, if not,
    a copy of the proposed by-laws; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} details of any arrangements necessary to perfect
    the amalgamation and to provide for the subsequent management and
    operation of the amalgamated corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If shares of one of the amalgamating corporations are
  held by or on behalf of another of the amalgamating corporations, the
  amalgamation agreement shall provide for the cancellation of such
  shares when the amalgamation becomes effective without any repayment
  of capital in respect thereof, and no provision shall be made in the
  agreement for the conversion of such shares into shares of the
  amalgamated corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 176
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shareholder approval

- **[[183]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  directors of each amalgamating corporation shall submit the
  amalgamation agreement for approval to a meeting of the holders of
  shares of the amalgamating corporation of which they are directors
  and, subject to subsection (4), to the holders of each class or series
  of such shares.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of meeting

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice of a meeting of shareholders complying with
  section 135 shall be sent in accordance with that section to each
  shareholder of each amalgamating corporation, and shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} include or be accompanied by a copy or summary of
    the amalgamation agreement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} state that a dissenting shareholder is entitled to
    be paid the fair value of their shares in accordance with section
    190, but failure to make that statement does not invalidate an
    amalgamation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to vote

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Each share of an amalgamating corporation carries the
  right to vote in respect of an amalgamation agreement whether or not
  it otherwise carries the right to vote.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class vote

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The holders of shares of a class or series of shares
  of each amalgamating corporation are entitled to vote separately as a
  class or series in respect of an amalgamation agreement if the
  amalgamation agreement contains a provision that, if contained in a
  proposed amendment to the articles, would entitle such holders to vote
  as a class or series under section 176.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shareholder approval

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), an amalgamation agreement
  is adopted when the shareholders of each amalgamating corporation have
  approved of the amalgamation by special resolutions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An amalgamation agreement may provide that at any
  time before the issue of a certificate of amalgamation the agreement
  may be terminated by the directors of an amalgamating corporation,
  notwithstanding approval of the agreement by the shareholders of all
  or any of the amalgamating corporations.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 183
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 87, 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vertical short-form amalgamation

- **[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  holding corporation and one or more of its subsidiary corporations may
  amalgamate and continue as one corporation without complying with
  sections 182 and 183 if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the amalgamation is approved by a resolution of the
    directors of each amalgamating corporation;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} all of the issued shares of each amalgamating
    subsidiary corporation are held by one or more of the other
    amalgamating corporations; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the resolutions provide that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the shares of each amalgamating subsidiary
      corporation shall be cancelled without any repayment of capital in
      respect thereof,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} except as may be prescribed, the articles of
      amalgamation shall be the same as the articles of the amalgamating
      holding corporation, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} no securities shall be issued by the
      amalgamated corporation in connection with the amalgamation and
      the stated capital of the amalgamated corporation shall be the
      same as the stated capital of the amalgamating holding
      corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Horizontal short-form amalgamation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Two or more wholly-owned subsidiary corporations of
  the same holding body corporate may amalgamate and continue as one
  corporation without complying with sections 182 and 183 if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the amalgamation is approved by a resolution of the
    directors of each amalgamating corporation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the resolutions provide that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the shares of all but one of the amalgamating
      subsidiary corporations shall be cancelled without any repayment
      of capital in respect thereof,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} except as may be prescribed, the articles of
      amalgamation shall be the same as the articles of the amalgamating
      subsidiary corporation whose shares are not cancelled, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the stated capital of the amalgamating
      subsidiary corporations whose shares are cancelled shall be added
      to the stated capital of the amalgamating subsidiary corporation
      whose shares are not cancelled.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 184
- 1994, c. 24, s. 20
- 2001, c. 14, s. 88
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sending of articles

- **[[185]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-185
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 183(6), after
  an amalgamation has been adopted under section 183 or approved under
  section 184, articles of amalgamation in the form that the Director
  fixes shall be sent to the Director together with the documents
  required by sections 19 and 106.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attached declarations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The articles of amalgamation shall have attached
  thereto a statutory declaration of a director or an officer of each
  amalgamating corporation that establishes to the satisfaction of the
  Director that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there are reasonable grounds for believing that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} each amalgamating corporation is and the
      amalgamated corporation will be able to pay its liabilities as
      they become due, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the realizable value of the amalgamated
      corporation's assets will not be less than the aggregate of its
      liabilities and stated capital of all classes; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there are reasonable grounds for believing that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} no creditor will be prejudiced by the
      amalgamation, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} adequate notice has been given to all known
      creditors of the amalgamating corporations and no creditor objects
      to the amalgamation otherwise than on grounds that are frivolous
      or vexatious.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adequate notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (2), adequate notice
  is given if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a notice in writing is sent to each known creditor
    having a claim against the corporation that exceeds one thousand
    dollars;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a notice is published once in a newspaper published
    or distributed in the place where the corporation has its registered
    office and reasonable notice thereof is given in each province where
    the corporation carries on business; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} each notice states that the corporation intends to
    amalgamate with one or more specified corporations in accordance
    with this Act and that a creditor of the corporation may object to
    the amalgamation within thirty days from the date of the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of amalgamation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of articles of amalgamation, the Director
  shall issue a certificate of amalgamation in accordance with section
  262.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 185
- 2001, c. 14, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

**[[186]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186 .sectionLabel}** On the date shown in a
certificate of amalgamation

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the amalgamation of the amalgamating corporations and
  their continuance as one corporation become effective;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the property of each amalgamating corporation
  continues to be the property of the amalgamated corporation;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the amalgamated corporation continues to be liable
  for the obligations of each amalgamating corporation;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} an existing cause of action, claim or liability to
  prosecution is unaffected;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} a civil, criminal or administrative action or
  proceeding pending by or against an amalgamating corporation may be
  continued to be prosecuted by or against the amalgamated corporation;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} a conviction against, or ruling, order or judgment in
  favour of or against, an amalgamating corporation may be enforced by
  or against the amalgamated corporation; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the articles of amalgamation are deemed to be the
  articles of incorporation of the amalgamated corporation and the
  certificate of amalgamation is deemed to be the certificate of
  incorporation of the amalgamated corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 180
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amalgamation under other federal Acts

- **[[186.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a
  corporation may not amalgamate with one or more bodies corporate
  pursuant to the [Bank Act](/eng/acts/B-1.01), the [Canada Cooperatives
  Act](/eng/acts/C-1.7), the [Cooperative Credit Associations
  Act](/eng/acts/C-41.01), the [Insurance Companies
  Act](/eng/acts/I-11.8) or the [Trust and Loan Companies
  Act](/eng/acts/T-19.8) unless the corporation is first authorized to
  do so by the shareholders in accordance with section 183.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short-form amalgamations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A corporation may not amalgamate with one or more
  bodies corporate pursuant to the provisions of one of the Acts
  referred to in subsection (1) respecting short-form amalgamations
  unless the corporation is first authorized to do so by the directors
  in accordance with section 184.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discontinuance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a notice satisfactory to the Director
  that a corporation has amalgamated pursuant to one of the Acts
  referred to in subsection (1), the Director shall file the notice and
  issue a certificate of discontinuance in accordance with section 262.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice deemed to be articles

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of section 262, a notice referred to
  in subsection (3) is deemed to be articles that are in the form that
  the Director fixes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Act ceases to apply

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} This Act ceases to apply to the corporation on the
  date shown in the certificate of discontinuance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, section 185 does not apply to
  a corporation that amalgamates pursuant to one of the Acts referred to
  in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1994, c. 24, s. 21
- 1998, c. 1, s. 380
- 2001, c. 14, s. 90
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuance (import)

- **[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  body corporate incorporated otherwise than by or under an Act of
  Parliament may, if so authorized by the laws of the jurisdiction where
  it is incorporated, apply to the Director for a certificate of
  continuance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendments in articles of continuance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A body corporate that applies for continuance under
  subsection (1) may, without so stating in its articles of continuance,
  effect by those articles any amendment to its Act of incorporation,
  articles, letters patent or memorandum or articles of association if
  the amendment is an amendment a corporation incorporated under this
  Act may make to its articles.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Articles of continuance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Articles of continuance in the form that the Director
  fixes shall be sent to the Director together with the documents
  required by sections 19 and 106.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of continuance

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of articles of continuance, the Director
  shall issue a certificate of continuance in accordance with section
  262.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On the date shown in the certificate of continuance

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the body corporate becomes a corporation to which
    this Act applies as if it had been incorporated under this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the articles of continuance are deemed to be the
    articles of incorporation of the continued corporation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the certificate of continuance is deemed to be the
    certificate of incorporation of the continued corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of certificate

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Director shall forthwith send a copy of the
  certificate of continuance to the appropriate official or public body
  in the jurisdiction in which continuance under this Act was
  authorized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights preserved

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} When a body corporate is continued as a corporation
  under this Act,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the property of the body corporate continues to be
    the property of the corporation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the corporation continues to be liable for the
    obligations of the body corporate;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an existing cause of action, claim or liability to
    prosecution is unaffected;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a civil, criminal or administrative action or
    proceeding pending by or against the body corporate may be continued
    to be prosecuted by or against the corporation; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a conviction against, or ruling, order or judgment
    in favour of or against, the body corporate may be enforced by or
    against the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issued shares

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (9) and 49(8), a share of a
  body corporate issued before the body corporate was continued under
  this Act is deemed to have been issued in compliance with this Act and
  with the provisions of the articles of continuance irrespective of
  whether the share is fully paid and irrespective of any designation,
  rights, privileges, restrictions or conditions set out on or referred
  to in the certificate representing the share. Continuance under this
  section does not deprive a holder of any right or privilege that the
  holder claims under, or relieve the holder of any liability in respect
  of, an issued share.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Convertible shares

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If a corporation continued under this Act had, before
  it was so continued, issued a share certificate in registered form
  that is convertible to bearer form, the corporation shall not, if a
  holder of such a share certificate exercises the conversion privilege
  attached to the certificate, issue a share certificate in bearer form.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
  [[share]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (8) and (9),
  [[share]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes an instrument referred to in
  subsection 29(1), a share warrant as defined in the [Canada
  Corporations Act](/eng/acts/C-1.8), chapter C-32 of the Revised
  Statutes of Canada, 1970, or a like instrument.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where continued reference to par value
  shares permissible

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} Where the Director determines, on the application of
  a body corporate, that it is not practicable to change a reference to
  the nominal or par value of shares of a class or series that the body
  corporate was authorized to issue before it was continued under this
  Act, the Director may, notwithstanding subsection 24(1), permit the
  body corporate to continue to refer in its articles to those shares,
  whether issued or unissued, as shares having a nominal or par value.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} A corporation shall set out in its articles the
  maximum number of shares of a class or series referred to in
  subsection (11) and may not amend its articles to increase that
  maximum number of shares or to change the nominal or par value of
  those shares.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 187
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 91, 135(E)
- 2018, c. 8, s. 25
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-187-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 187"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuance --- other jurisdictions

- **[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (10), a
  corporation may apply to the appropriate official or public body of
  another jurisdiction requesting that the corporation be continued as
  if it had been incorporated under the laws of that other jurisdiction
  if the corporation

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is authorized by the shareholders in accordance
    with this section to make the application; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} establishes to the satisfaction of the Director
    that its proposed continuance in the other jurisdiction will not
    adversely affect creditors or shareholders of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuance --- other federal Acts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A corporation that is authorized by the shareholders
  in accordance with this section may apply to the appropriate Minister
  for its continuance under the [Bank Act](/eng/acts/B-1.01), the
  [Canada Cooperatives Act](/eng/acts/C-1.7), the [Cooperative Credit
  Associations Act](/eng/acts/C-41.01), the [Insurance Companies
  Act](/eng/acts/I-11.8) or the [Trust and Loan Companies
  Act](/eng/acts/T-19.8).

- [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 14, s. 92\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of meeting

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A notice of a meeting of shareholders complying with
  section 135 shall be sent in accordance with that section to each
  shareholder and shall state that a dissenting shareholder is entitled
  to be paid the fair value of their shares in accordance with section
  190, but failure to make that statement does not invalidate a
  discontinuance under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to vote

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Each share of the corporation carries the right to
  vote in respect of a continuance whether or not it otherwise carries
  the right to vote.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shareholder approval

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An application for continuance becomes authorized
  when the shareholders voting thereon have approved of the continuance
  by a special resolution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The directors of a corporation may, if authorized by
  the shareholders at the time of approving an application for
  continuance under this section, abandon the application without
  further approval of the shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discontinuance

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a notice satisfactory to the Director
  that the corporation has been continued under the laws of another
  jurisdiction or under one of the Acts referred to in subsection (2.1),
  the Director shall file the notice and issue a certificate of
  discontinuance in accordance with section 262.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice deemed to be articles

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of section 262, a notice referred to
  in subsection (7) is deemed to be articles that are in the form that
  the Director fixes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights preserved

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} This Act ceases to apply to the corporation on the
  date shown in the certificate of discontinuance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} A corporation shall not be continued as a body
  corporate under the laws of another jurisdiction unless those laws
  provide in effect that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the property of the corporation continues to be the
    property of the body corporate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the body corporate continues to be liable for the
    obligations of the corporation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an existing cause of action, claim or liability to
    prosecution is unaffected;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a civil, criminal or administrative action or
    proceeding pending by or against the corporation may be continued to
    be prosecuted by or against the body corporate; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a conviction against, or ruling, order or judgment
    in favour of or against, the corporation may be enforced by or
    against the body corporate.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 188
- 1991, c. 45, s. 555, c. 46, s. 596, c. 47, s. 723
- 1994, c. 24, s. 22
- 1998, c. 1, s. 381
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 92, 135(E)
- 2007, c. 6, s. 400
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-188-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 188"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Borrowing powers

- **[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the articles or by-laws of or
  a unanimous shareholder agreement relating to a corporation otherwise
  provide, the directors of a corporation may, without authorization of
  the shareholders,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} borrow money on the credit of the corporation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} issue, reissue, sell, pledge or hypothecate debt
    obligations of the corporation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} give a guarantee on behalf of the corporation to
    secure performance of an obligation of any person; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} mortgage, hypothecate, pledge or otherwise create a
    security interest in all or any property of the corporation, owned
    or subsequently acquired, to secure any obligation of the
    corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation of borrowing powers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection 115(3) and paragraph
  121(a), unless the articles or by-laws of or a unanimous shareholder
  agreement relating to a corporation otherwise provide, the directors
  may, by resolution, delegate the powers referred to in subsection (1)
  to a director, a committee of directors or an officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extraordinary sale, lease or exchange

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A sale, lease or exchange of all or substantially all
  the property of a corporation other than in the ordinary course of
  business of the corporation requires the approval of the shareholders
  in accordance with subsections (4) to (8).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of meeting

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A notice of a meeting of shareholders complying with
  section 135 shall be sent in accordance with that section to each
  shareholder and shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} include or be accompanied by a copy or summary of
    the agreement of sale, lease or exchange; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} state that a dissenting shareholder is entitled to
    be paid the fair value of their shares in accordance with section
    190, but failure to make that statement does not invalidate a sale,
    lease or exchange referred to in subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shareholder approval

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} At the meeting referred to in subsection (4), the
  shareholders may authorize the sale, lease or exchange and may fix or
  authorize the directors to fix any of the terms and conditions
  thereof.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to vote

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Each share of the corporation carries the right to
  vote in respect of a sale, lease or exchange referred to in
  subsection (3) whether or not it otherwise carries the right to vote.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class vote

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The holders of shares of a class or series of shares
  of the corporation are entitled to vote separately as a class or
  series in respect of a sale, lease or exchange referred to in
  subsection (3) only if such class or series is affected by the sale,
  lease or exchange in a manner different from the shares of another
  class or series.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shareholder approval

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A sale, lease or exchange referred to in
  subsection (3) is adopted when the holders of each class or series
  entitled to vote thereon have approved of the sale, lease or exchange
  by a special resolution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The directors of a corporation may, if authorized by
  the shareholders approving a proposed sale, lease or exchange, and
  subject to the rights of third parties, abandon the sale, lease or
  exchange without further approval of the shareholders.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 189
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 93, 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 59(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-189-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 189"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to dissent

- **[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 191 and 241, a
  holder of shares of any class of a corporation may dissent if the
  corporation is subject to an order under paragraph 192(4)(d) that
  affects the holder or if the corporation resolves to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} amend its articles under section 173 or 174 to add,
    change or remove any provisions restricting or constraining the
    issue, transfer or ownership of shares of that class;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} amend its articles under section 173 to add, change
    or remove any restriction on the business or businesses that the
    corporation may carry on;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} amalgamate otherwise than under section 184;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} be continued under section 188;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} sell, lease or exchange all or substantially all
    its property under subsection 189(3); or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} carry out a going-private transaction or a
    squeeze-out transaction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further right

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A holder of shares of any class or series of shares
  entitled to vote under section 176 may dissent if the corporation
  resolves to amend its articles in a manner described in that section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If one class of shares

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The right to dissent described in subsection (2)
  applies even if there is only one class of shares.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment for shares

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In addition to any other right the shareholder may
  have, but subject to subsection (26), a shareholder who complies with
  this section is entitled, when the action approved by the resolution
  from which the shareholder dissents or an order made under subsection
  192(4) becomes effective, to be paid by the corporation the fair value
  of the shares in respect of which the shareholder dissents, determined
  as of the close of business on the day before the resolution was
  adopted or the order was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No partial dissent

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A dissenting shareholder may only claim under this
  section with respect to all the shares of a class held on behalf of
  any one beneficial owner and registered in the name of the dissenting
  shareholder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objection

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A dissenting shareholder shall send to the
  corporation, at or before any meeting of shareholders at which a
  resolution referred to in subsection (1) or (2) is to be voted on, a
  written objection to the resolution, unless the corporation did not
  give notice to the shareholder of the purpose of the meeting and of
  their right to dissent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of resolution

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The corporation shall, within ten days after the
  shareholders adopt the resolution, send to each shareholder who has
  filed the objection referred to in subsection (5) notice that the
  resolution has been adopted, but such notice is not required to be
  sent to any shareholder who voted for the resolution or who has
  withdrawn their objection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Demand for payment

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A dissenting shareholder shall, within twenty days
  after receiving a notice under subsection (6) or, if the shareholder
  does not receive such notice, within twenty days after learning that
