  the resolution has been adopted, send to the corporation a written
  notice containing

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the shareholder's name and address;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number and class of shares in respect of which
    the shareholder dissents; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a demand for payment of the fair value of such
    shares.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Share certificate

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A dissenting shareholder shall, within thirty days
  after sending a notice under subsection (7), send the certificates
  representing the shares in respect of which the shareholder dissents
  to the corporation or its transfer agent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forfeiture

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} A dissenting shareholder who fails to comply with
  subsection (8) has no right to make a claim under this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Endorsing certificate

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} A corporation or its transfer agent shall endorse on
  any share certificate received under subsection (8) a notice that the
  holder is a dissenting shareholder under this section and shall
  forthwith return the share certificates to the dissenting shareholder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of rights

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} On sending a notice under subsection (7), a
  dissenting shareholder ceases to have any rights as a shareholder
  other than to be paid the fair value of their shares as determined
  under this section except where

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the shareholder withdraws that notice before the
    corporation makes an offer under subsection (12),

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the corporation fails to make an offer in
    accordance with subsection (12) and the shareholder withdraws the
    notice, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the directors revoke a resolution to amend the
    articles under subsection 173(2) or 174(5), terminate an
    amalgamation agreement under subsection 183(6) or an application for
    continuance under subsection 188(6), or abandon a sale, lease or
    exchange under subsection 189(9),

  in which case the shareholder's rights are reinstated as of the date
  the notice was sent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offer to pay

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} A corporation shall, not later than seven days after
  the later of the day on which the action approved by the resolution is
  effective or the day the corporation received the notice referred to
  in subsection (7), send to each dissenting shareholder who has sent
  such notice

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a written offer to pay for their shares in an
    amount considered by the directors of the corporation to be the fair
    value, accompanied by a statement showing how the fair value was
    determined; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if subsection (26) applies, a notification that it
    is unable lawfully to pay dissenting shareholders for their shares.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Same terms

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} Every offer made under subsection (12) for shares of
  the same class or series shall be on the same terms.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment

  [(14)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (26), a corporation shall pay
  for the shares of a dissenting shareholder within ten days after an
  offer made under subsection (12) has been accepted, but any such offer
  lapses if the corporation does not receive an acceptance thereof
  within thirty days after the offer has been made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corporation may apply to court

  [(15)]{.lawlabel} Where a corporation fails to make an offer under
  subsection (12), or if a dissenting shareholder fails to accept an
  offer, the corporation may, within fifty days after the action
  approved by the resolution is effective or within such further period
  as a court may allow, apply to a court to fix a fair value for the
  shares of any dissenting shareholder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shareholder application to court

  [(16)]{.lawlabel} If a corporation fails to apply to a court under
  subsection (15), a dissenting shareholder may apply to a court for the
  same purpose within a further period of twenty days or within such
  further period as a court may allow.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Venue

  [(17)]{.lawlabel} An application under subsection (15) or (16) shall
  be made to a court having jurisdiction in the place where the
  corporation has its registered office or in the province where the
  dissenting shareholder resides if the corporation carries on business
  in that province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No security for costs

  [(18)]{.lawlabel} A dissenting shareholder is not required to give
  security for costs in an application made under subsection (15) or
  (16).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Parties

  [(19)]{.lawlabel} On an application to a court under subsection (15)
  or (16),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all dissenting shareholders whose shares have not
    been purchased by the corporation shall be joined as parties and are
    bound by the decision of the court; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the corporation shall notify each affected
    dissenting shareholder of the date, place and consequences of the
    application and of their right to appear and be heard in person or
    by counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of court

  [(20)]{.lawlabel} On an application to a court under subsection (15)
  or (16), the court may determine whether any other person is a
  dissenting shareholder who should be joined as a party, and the court
  shall then fix a fair value for the shares of all dissenting
  shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appraisers

  [(21)]{.lawlabel} A court may in its discretion appoint one or more
  appraisers to assist the court to fix a fair value for the shares of
  the dissenting shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Final order

  [(22)]{.lawlabel} The final order of a court shall be rendered against
  the corporation in favour of each dissenting shareholder and for the
  amount of the shares as fixed by the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest

  [(23)]{.lawlabel} A court may in its discretion allow a reasonable
  rate of interest on the amount payable to each dissenting shareholder
  from the date the action approved by the resolution is effective until
  the date of payment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice that subsection (26) applies

  [(24)]{.lawlabel} If subsection (26) applies, the corporation shall,
  within ten days after the pronouncement of an order under subsection
  (22), notify each dissenting shareholder that it is unable lawfully to
  pay dissenting shareholders for their shares.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect where subsection (26) applies

  [(25)]{.lawlabel} If subsection (26) applies, a dissenting
  shareholder, by written notice delivered to the corporation within
  thirty days after receiving a notice under subsection (24), may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} withdraw their notice of dissent, in which case the
    corporation is deemed to consent to the withdrawal and the
    shareholder is reinstated to their full rights as a shareholder; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} retain a status as a claimant against the
    corporation, to be paid as soon as the corporation is lawfully able
    to do so or, in a liquidation, to be ranked subordinate to the
    rights of creditors of the corporation but in priority to its
    shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(26)]{.lawlabel} A corporation shall not make a payment to a
  dissenting shareholder under this section if there are reasonable
  grounds for believing that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the corporation is or would after the payment be
    unable to pay its liabilities as they become due; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the realizable value of the corporation's assets
    would thereby be less than the aggregate of its liabilities.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 190
- 1994, c. 24, s. 23
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 94, 134(F), 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 60(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-190-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 190"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[reorganization]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[reorganization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a court
  order made under

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} section 241;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the [Bankruptcy and Insolvency Act](/eng/acts/B-3)
    approving a proposal; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any other Act of Parliament that affects the rights
    among the corporation, its shareholders and creditors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a corporation is subject to an order referred to
  in subsection (1), its articles may be amended by such order to effect
  any change that might lawfully be made by an amendment under section
  173.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further powers

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a court makes an order referred to in subsection
  (1), the court may also

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} authorize the issue of debt obligations of the
    corporation, whether or not convertible into shares of any class or
    having attached any rights or options to acquire shares of any
    class, and fix the terms thereof; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} appoint directors in place of or in addition to all
    or any of the directors then in office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Articles of reorganization

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} After an order referred to in subsection (1) has been
  made, articles of reorganization in the form that the Director fixes
  shall be sent to the Director together with the documents required by
  sections 19 and 113, if applicable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of reorganization

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of articles of reorganization, the
  Director shall issue a certificate of amendment in accordance with
  section 262.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A reorganization becomes effective on the date shown
  in the certificate of amendment and the articles of incorporation are
  amended accordingly.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No dissent

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A shareholder is not entitled to dissent under
  section 190 if an amendment to the articles of incorporation is
  effected under this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 191
- 1992, c. 27, s. 90
- 2001, c. 14, s. 95
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[arrangement]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[arrangement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an amendment to the articles of a corporation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an amalgamation of two or more corporations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an amalgamation of a body corporate with a
    corporation that results in an amalgamated corporation subject to
    this Act;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a division of the business carried on by a
    corporation;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a transfer of all or substantially all the property
    of a corporation to another body corporate in exchange for property,
    money or securities of the body corporate;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} an exchange of securities of a corporation for
    property, money or other securities of the corporation or property,
    money or securities of another body corporate;

  - [(f.1)]{.lawlabel} a going-private transaction or a squeeze-out
    transaction in relation to a corporation;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a liquidation and dissolution of a corporation; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} any combination of the foregoing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where corporation insolvent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, a corporation is
  insolvent

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where it is unable to pay its liabilities as they
    become due; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the realizable value of the assets of the
    corporation are less than the aggregate of its liabilities and
    stated capital of all classes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to court for approval of
  arrangement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where it is not practicable for a corporation that is
  not insolvent to effect a fundamental change in the nature of an
  arrangement under any other provision of this Act, the corporation may
  apply to a court for an order approving an arrangement proposed by the
  corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of court

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In connection with an application under this section,
  the court may make any interim or final order it thinks fit including,
  without limiting the generality of the foregoing,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an order determining the notice to be given to any
    interested person or dispensing with notice to any person other than
    the Director;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order appointing counsel, at the expense of the
    corporation, to represent the interests of the shareholders;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an order requiring a corporation to call, hold and
    conduct a meeting of holders of securities or options or rights to
    acquire securities in such manner as the court directs;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an order permitting a shareholder to dissent under
    section 190; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an order approving an arrangement as proposed by
    the corporation or as amended in any manner the court may direct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Director

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An applicant for any interim or final order under
  this section shall give the Director notice of the application and the
  Director is entitled to appear and be heard in person or by counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Articles of arrangement

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} After an order referred to in paragraph (4)(e) has
  been made, articles of arrangement in the form that the Director fixes
  shall be sent to the Director together with the documents required by
  sections 19 and 113, if applicable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of arrangement

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of articles of arrangement, the Director
  shall issue a certificate of arrangement in accordance with section
  262.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} An arrangement becomes effective on the date shown in
  the certificate of arrangement.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 192
- 1994, c. 24, s. 24
- 2001, c. 14, s. 96
:::

## [PART XVI]{.HLabel1}[Going-private Transactions and Squeeze-out Transactions]{.HTitleText1} {#h-111183 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Going-private transactions

**[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193 .sectionLabel}** A corporation may carry
out a going-private transaction. However, if there are any applicable
provincial securities laws, a corporation may not carry out a
going-private transaction unless the corporation complies with those
laws.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 193
- 2001, c. 14, s. 97
- 2018, c. 8, s. 26(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-193-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 193"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Squeeze-out transactions

**[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194 .sectionLabel}** A corporation may not
carry out a squeeze-out transaction unless, in addition to any approval
by holders of shares required by or under this Act or the articles of
the corporation, the transaction is approved by ordinary resolution of
the holders of each class of shares that are affected by the
transaction, voting separately, whether or not the shares otherwise
carry the right to vote. However, the following do not have the right to
vote on the resolution:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} affiliates of the corporation; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} holders of shares that would, following the
  squeeze-out transaction, be entitled to consideration of greater value
  or to superior rights or privileges than those available to other
  holders of shares of the same class.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 194
- 2001, c. 14, s. 97
:::

**[[195 to 205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195_to_205
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 14, s. 97\]]{.Repealed}

## [PART XVII]{.HLabel1}[Compulsory and Compelled Acquisitions]{.HTitleText1} {#h-111195 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  definitions in this subsection apply in this Part.

  [[dissenting offeree]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[dissenting offeree]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, where a
      take-over bid is made for all the shares of a class of shares, a
      holder of a share of that class who does not accept the take-over
      bid and includes a subsequent holder of that share who acquires it
      from the first mentioned holder; ([pollicité
      dissident]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[offer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[offer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes an invitation to make an
      offer. ([pollicitation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[offeree]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[offeree]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person to whom a take-over
      bid is made. ([pollicité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[offeree corporation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[offeree corporation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a distributing
      corporation whose shares are the object of a take-over
      bid. ([société pollicitée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[offeror]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[offeror]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person, other than an
      agent or mandatary, who makes a take-over bid, and includes two or
      more persons who, directly or indirectly,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} make take-over bids jointly or in concert; or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} intend to exercise jointly or in concert voting
        rights attached to shares for which a take-over bid is
        made. ([pollicitant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[share]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[share]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a share, with or without
      voting rights, and includes

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a security currently convertible into such a
        share; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} currently exercisable options and rights to
        acquire such a share or such a convertible
        security. ([action]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[take-over bid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[take-over bid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an offer made by an
      offeror to shareholders of a distributing corporation at
      approximately the same time to acquire all of the shares of a
      class of issued shares, and includes an offer made by a
      distributing corporation to repurchase all of the shares of a
      class of its shares. ([offre d'achat visant à la
      mainmise]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to acquire

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If within one hundred and twenty days after the date
  of a take-over bid the bid is accepted by the holders of not less than
  ninety per cent of the shares of any class of shares to which the
  take-over bid relates, other than shares held at the date of the
  take-over bid by or on behalf of the offeror or an affiliate or
  associate of the offeror, the offeror is entitled, on complying with
  this section, to acquire the shares held by the dissenting offerees.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An offeror may acquire shares held by a dissenting
  offeree by sending by registered mail within sixty days after the date
  of termination of the take-over bid and in any event within one
  hundred and eighty days after the date of the take-over bid, an
  offeror's notice to each dissenting offeree and to the Director
  stating that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the offerees holding not less than ninety per cent
    of the shares to which the bid relates accepted the take-over bid;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the offeror is bound to take up and pay for or has
    taken up and paid for the shares of the offerees who accepted the
    take-over bid;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a dissenting offeree is required to elect

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to transfer their shares to the offeror on the
      terms on which the offeror acquired the shares of the offerees who
      accepted the take-over bid, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to demand payment of the fair value of the
      shares in accordance with subsections (9) to (18) by notifying the
      offeror within twenty days after receiving the offeror's notice;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a dissenting offeree who does not notify the
    offeror in accordance with subparagraph (5)(b)(ii) is deemed to have
    elected to transfer the shares to the offeror on the same terms that
    the offeror acquired the shares from the offerees who accepted the
    take-over bid; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a dissenting offeree must send their shares to
    which the take-over bid relates to the offeree corporation within
    twenty days after receiving the offeror's notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of adverse claim

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Concurrently with sending the offeror's notice under
  subsection (3), the offeror shall send to the offeree corporation a
  notice of adverse claim in accordance with section 78 with respect to
  each share held by a dissenting offeree.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Share certificate

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A dissenting offeree to whom an offeror's notice is
  sent under subsection (3) shall, within twenty days after receiving
  the notice,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} send the share certificates of the class of shares
    to which the take-over bid relates to the offeree corporation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} elect

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to transfer the shares to the offeror on the
      terms on which the offeror acquired the shares of the offerees who
      accepted the take-over bid, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to demand payment of the fair value of the
      shares in accordance with subsections (9) to (18) by notifying the
      offeror within those twenty days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed election

  [(5.1)]{.lawlabel} A dissenting offeree who does not notify the
  offeror in accordance with subparagraph (5)(b)(ii) is deemed to have
  elected to transfer the shares to the offeror on the same terms on
  which the offeror acquired the shares from the offerees who accepted
  the take-over bid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Within twenty days after the offeror sends an
  offeror's notice under subsection (3), the offeror shall pay or
  transfer to the offeree corporation the amount of money or other
  consideration that the offeror would have had to pay or transfer to a
  dissenting offeree if the dissenting offeree had elected to accept the
  take-over bid under subparagraph (5)(b)(i).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The offeree corporation is deemed to hold in trust
  for the dissenting shareholders the money or other consideration it
  receives under subsection (6), and the offeree corporation shall
  deposit the money in a separate account in a bank or other body
  corporate any of whose deposits are insured by the Canada Deposit
  Insurance Corporation or guaranteed by the Quebec Deposit Insurance
  Board, and shall place the other consideration in the custody of a
  bank or such other body corporate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When corporation is offeror

  [(7.1)]{.lawlabel} A corporation that is an offeror making a take-over
  bid to repurchase all of the shares of a class of its shares is deemed
  to hold in trust for the dissenting shareholders the money and other
  consideration that it would have had to pay or transfer to a
  dissenting offeree if the dissenting offeree had elected to accept the
  take-over bid under subparagraph (5)(b)(i), and the corporation shall,
  within twenty days after a notice is sent under subsection (3),
  deposit the money in a separate account in a bank or other body
  corporate any of whose deposits are insured by the Canada Deposit
  Insurance Corporation or guaranteed by the Quebec Deposit Insurance
  Board, and shall place the other consideration in the custody of a
  bank or such other body corporate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of offeree corporation

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Within thirty days after the offeror sends a notice
  under subsection (3), the offeree corporation shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the payment or transfer required by
    subsection (6) is made, issue to the offeror a share certificate in
    respect of the shares that were held by dissenting offerees;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} give to each dissenting offeree who elects to
    accept the take-over bid terms under subparagraph (5)(b)(i) and who
    sends share certificates as required by paragraph (5)(a) the money
    or other consideration to which the offeree is entitled,
    disregarding fractional shares, which may be paid for in money; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the payment or transfer required by
    subsection (6) is made and the money or other consideration is
    deposited as required by subsection (7) or (7.1), send to each
    dissenting shareholder who has not sent share certificates as
    required by paragraph (5)(a) a notice stating that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the dissenting shareholder's shares have been
      cancelled,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the offeree corporation or some designated
      person holds in trust for the dissenting shareholder the money or
      other consideration to which that shareholder is entitled as
      payment for or in exchange for the shares, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the offeree corporation will, subject to
      subsections (9) to (18), send that money or other consideration to
      that shareholder without delay after receiving the shares.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to court

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If a dissenting offeree has elected to demand payment
  of the fair value of the shares under subparagraph (5)(b)(ii), the
  offeror may, within twenty days after it has paid the money or
  transferred the other consideration under subsection (6), apply to a
  court to fix the fair value of the shares of that dissenting offeree.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} If an offeror fails to apply to a court under
  subsection (9), a dissenting offeree may apply to a court for the same
  purpose within a further period of twenty days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Status of dissenter if no court
  application

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} Where no application is made to a court under
  subsection (10) within the period set out in that subsection, a
  dissenting offeree is deemed to have elected to transfer their shares
  to the offeror on the same terms that the offeror acquired the shares
  from the offerees who accepted the take-over bid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Venue

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} An application under subsection (9) or (10) shall be
  made to a court having jurisdiction in the place where the corporation
  has its registered office or in the province where the dissenting
  offeree resides if the corporation carries on business in that
  province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No security for costs

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} A dissenting offeree is not required to give
  security for costs in an application made under subsection (9) or
  (10).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Parties

  [(14)]{.lawlabel} On an application under subsection (9) or (10)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all dissenting offerees referred to in subparagraph
    (5)(b)(ii) whose shares have not been acquired by the offeror shall
    be joined as parties and are bound by the decision of the court; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the offeror shall notify each affected dissenting
    offeree of the date, place and consequences of the application and
    of their right to appear and be heard in person or by counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of court

  [(15)]{.lawlabel} On an application to a court under subsection (9) or
  (10), the court may determine whether any other person is a dissenting
  offeree who should be joined as a party, and the court shall then fix
  a fair value for the shares of all dissenting offerees.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appraisers

  [(16)]{.lawlabel} A court may in its discretion appoint one or more
  appraisers to assist the court to fix a fair value for the shares of a
  dissenting offeree.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Final order

  [(17)]{.lawlabel} The final order of the court shall be made against
  the offeror in favour of each dissenting offeree and for the amount
  for the shares as fixed by the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional powers

  [(18)]{.lawlabel} In connection with proceedings under this section, a
  court may make any order it thinks fit and, without limiting the
  generality of the foregoing, it may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} fix the amount of money or other consideration that
    is required to be held in trust under subsection (7) or (7.1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} order that that money or other consideration be
    held in trust by a person other than the offeree corporation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} allow a reasonable rate of interest on the amount
    payable to each dissenting offeree from the date they send or
    deliver their share certificates under subsection (5) until the date
    of payment; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} order that any money payable to a shareholder who
    cannot be found be paid to the Receiver General and subsection
    227(3) applies in respect thereof.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 206
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 99, 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 61
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-206-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 206"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation to acquire shares

- **[[206.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a shareholder holding shares of a
  distributing corporation does not receive an offeror's notice under
  subsection 206(3), the shareholder may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} within ninety days after the date of termination of
    the take-over bid, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the shareholder did not receive an offer
    pursuant to the take-over bid, within ninety days after the later of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date of termination of the take-over bid, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the shareholder learned of the
      take-over bid,

  require the offeror to acquire those shares.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a shareholder requires the offeror to acquire
  shares under subsection (1), the offeror shall acquire the shares on
  the same terms under which the offeror acquired or will acquire the
  shares of the offerees who accepted the take-over bid.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 14, s. 100
:::

## [PART XVIII]{.HLabel1}[Liquidation and Dissolution]{.HTitleText1} {#h-111285 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,
[[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a court having jurisdiction in the
place where the corporation has its registered office.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 200
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of Part

- **[[208]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Part, other than sections 209 and 212, does not apply to a corporation
  that is an [[insolvent person]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} or a
  [[bankrupt]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as those terms are defined in
  section 2 of the [Bankruptcy and Insolvency Act](/eng/acts/B-3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staying proceedings

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any proceedings taken under this Part to dissolve or
  to liquidate and dissolve a corporation shall be stayed if the
  corporation is at any time found, in a proceeding under the
  [Bankruptcy and Insolvency Act](/eng/acts/B-3), to be an [[insolvent
  person]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in section 2 of that Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 208
- 1992, c. 27, s. 90
- 2001, c. 14, s. 101
- 2018, c. 8, s. 27
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-208-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 208"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revival

- **[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  a corporation or other body corporate is dissolved under this Part,
  section 268 of this Act, section 261 of the [Canada Business
  Corporations Act](/eng/acts/C-44), chapter 33 of the Statutes of
  Canada, 1974-75-76, or subsection 297(6) of the [Canada Not-for-profit
  Corporations Act](/eng/acts/C-7.75), any interested person may apply
  to the Director to have the dissolved corporation or other body
  corporate revived as a corporation under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Articles of revival

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Articles of revival in the form that the Director
  fixes shall be sent to the Director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of revival

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of articles of revival, the Director shall
  issue a certificate of revival in accordance with section 262, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the dissolved corporation or other body corporate
    has fulfilled all conditions precedent that the Director considers
    reasonable; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is no valid reason for refusing to issue the
    certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Date of revival

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} The dissolved corporation or other body corporate
  is revived as a corporation under this Act on the date shown on the
  certificate of revival.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights and obligations preserved

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any reasonable terms that may be imposed
  by the Director, to the rights acquired by any person after its
  dissolution and to any changes to the internal affairs of the
  corporation or other body corporate after its dissolution, the revived
  corporation is, in the same manner and to the same extent as if it had
  not been dissolved,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} restored to its previous position in law, including
    the restoration of any rights and privileges whether arising before
    its dissolution or after its dissolution and before its revival; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} liable for the obligations that it would have had
    if it had not been dissolved whether they arise before its
    dissolution or after its dissolution and before its revival.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Legal actions

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Any legal action respecting the affairs of a revived
  corporation taken between the time of its dissolution and its revival
  is valid and effective.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[interested
  person]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[interested
  person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a shareholder, a director, an officer, an employee
    and a creditor of the dissolved corporation or other body corporate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who has a contract --- other than, in
    Quebec, a contract by gratuitous title --- with the dissolved
    corporation or other body corporate;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person who, although at the time of dissolution
    of the corporation or other body corporate was not a person
    described in paragraph (a), would be such a person if a certificate
    of revival is issued under this section; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a trustee in bankruptcy or liquidator for the
    dissolved corporation or other body corporate.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 209
- 2001, c. 14, s. 102
- 2009, c. 23, s. 310
- 2018, c. 8, s. 28
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-209-20100312.html "Link to previous version of section 209"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissolution before commencing business

- **[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  corporation that has not issued any shares may be dissolved at any
  time by resolution of all the directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissolution if no property

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A corporation that has no property and no liabilities
  may be dissolved by special resolution of the shareholders or, where
  it has issued more than one class of shares, by special resolutions of
  the holders of each class whether or not they are otherwise entitled
  to vote.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissolution where property disposed of

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A corporation that has property or liabilities or
  both may be dissolved by special resolution of the shareholders or,
  where it has issued more than one class of shares, by special
  resolutions of the holders of each class whether or not they are
  otherwise entitled to vote, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by the special resolution or resolutions the
    shareholders authorize the directors to cause the corporation to
    distribute any property and discharge any liabilities; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the corporation has distributed any property and
    discharged any liabilities before it sends articles of dissolution
    to the Director pursuant to subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Articles of dissolution

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Articles of dissolution in the form that the Director
  fixes shall be sent to the Director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of dissolution

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of articles of dissolution, the Director
  shall issue a certificate of dissolution in accordance with section
  262.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The corporation ceases to exist on the date shown in
  the certificate of dissolution.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 210
- 2001, c. 14, s. 103
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proposing liquidation and dissolution

- **[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  directors may propose, or a shareholder who is entitled to vote at an
  annual meeting of shareholders may, in accordance with section 137,
  make a proposal for, the voluntary liquidation and dissolution of a
  corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of meeting

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notice of any meeting of shareholders at which
  voluntary liquidation and dissolution is to be proposed shall set out
  the terms thereof.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shareholders resolution

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A corporation may liquidate and dissolve by special
  resolution of the shareholders or, where the corporation has issued
  more than one class of shares, by special resolutions of the holders
  of each class whether or not they are otherwise entitled to vote.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement of intent to dissolve

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A statement of intent to dissolve in the form that
  the Director fixes shall be sent to the Director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of intent to dissolve

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a statement of intent to dissolve, the
  Director shall issue a certificate of intent to dissolve in accordance
  with section 262.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} On issue of a certificate of intent to dissolve, the
  corporation shall cease to carry on business except to the extent
  necessary for the liquidation, but its corporate existence continues
  until the Director issues a certificate of dissolution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liquidation

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} After issue of a certificate of intent to dissolve,
  the corporation shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} immediately cause notice thereof to be sent to each
    known creditor of the corporation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} without delay take reasonable steps to give notice
    of it in each province in Canada where the corporation was carrying
    on business at the time it sent the statement of intent to dissolve
    to the Director;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} proceed to collect its property, to dispose of
    properties that are not to be distributed in kind to its
    shareholders, to discharge all its obligations and to do all other
    acts required to liquidate its business; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} after giving the notice required under
    paragraphs (a) and (b) and adequately providing for the payment or
    discharge of all its obligations, distribute its remaining property,
    either in money or in kind, among its shareholders according to
    their respective rights.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Supervision by court

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The Director or any interested person may, at any
  time during the liquidation of a corporation, apply to a court for an
  order that the liquidation be continued under the supervision of the
  court as provided in this Part, and on such application the court may
  so order and make any further order it thinks fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Director

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} An applicant under this section shall give the
  Director notice of the application, and the Director is entitled to
  appear and be heard in person or by counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} At any time after issue of a certificate of intent
  to dissolve and before issue of a certificate of dissolution, a
  certificate of intent to dissolve may be revoked by sending to the
  Director a statement of revocation of intent to dissolve in the form
  that the Director fixes, if such revocation is approved in the same
  manner as the resolution under subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of revocation of intent to
  dissolve

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a statement of revocation of intent to
  dissolve, the Director shall issue a certificate of revocation of
  intent to dissolve in accordance with section 262.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} On the date shown in the certificate of revocation
  of intent to dissolve, the revocation is effective and the corporation
  may continue to carry on its business or businesses.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to dissolve

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} If a certificate of intent to dissolve has not been
  revoked and the corporation has complied with subsection (7), the
  corporation shall prepare articles of dissolution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Articles of dissolution

  [(14)]{.lawlabel} Articles of dissolution in the form that the
  Director fixes shall be sent to the Director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of dissolution

  [(15)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of articles of dissolution, the Director
  shall issue a certificate of dissolution in accordance with section
  262.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

  [(16)]{.lawlabel} The corporation ceases to exist on the date shown in
  the certificate of dissolution.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 211
- 2001, c. 14, s. 104
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissolution by Director

- **[[212]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-212
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  the Director may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} dissolve a corporation by issuing a certificate of
    dissolution under this section if the corporation

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has not commenced business within three years
      after the date shown in its certificate of incorporation,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has not carried on its business for three
      consecutive years,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} is in default for a period of one year in
      sending to the Director any fee or any notice, document or other
      information required by this Act, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} does not have any directors or is in the
      situation described in subsection 109(4); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} apply to a court for an order dissolving the
    corporation, in which case section 217 applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Director shall not dissolve a corporation under
  this section until the Director has

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} given one hundred and twenty days notice of the
    decision to dissolve the corporation to the corporation and to each
    director thereof; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} published notice of that decision in a publication
    generally available to the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of dissolution

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless cause to the contrary has been shown or an
  order has been made by a court under section 246, the Director may,
  after the expiration of the period referred to in subsection (2),
  issue a certificate of dissolution in the form that the Director
  fixes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of incorporation fee or sending
  of information

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} Despite anything in this section, the Director may
  dissolve a corporation by issuing a certificate of dissolution if the
  required fee for the issuance of a certificate of incorporation has
  not been paid or if the corporation has not complied with subsection
  21.21(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The corporation ceases to exist on the date shown in
  the certificate of dissolution.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 212
- 1994, c. 24, s. 25
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 105, 135(E)
- 2018, c. 8, s. 29(F)
- [2023, c. 29, s. 6](#1426312-1438953){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1426312-1438953.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-212-20180501.html "Link to previous version of section 212"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grounds for dissolution

- **[[213]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-213 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Director or any interested person may apply to a court for an order
  dissolving a corporation if the corporation has

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} failed for two or more consecutive years to comply
    with the requirements of this Act with respect to the holding of
    annual meetings of shareholders;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} contravened subsection 16(2) or section 21, 157 or
    159; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} procured any certificate under this Act by
    misrepresentation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Director

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An applicant under this section shall give the
  Director notice of the application, and the Director is entitled to
  appear and be heard in person or by counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissolution order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On an application under this section or section 212,
  the court may order that the corporation be dissolved or that the
  corporation be liquidated and dissolved under the supervision of the
  court, and the court may make any other order it thinks fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of an order under this section, section
  212 or 214, the Director shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the order is to dissolve the corporation, issue
    a certificate of dissolution in the form that the Director fixes; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the order is to liquidate and dissolve the
    corporation under the supervision of the court, issue a certificate
    of intent to dissolve in the form that the Director fixes and
    publish notice of the order in a publication generally available to
    the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The corporation ceases to exist on the date shown in
  the certificate of dissolution.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 213
- 2001, c. 14, s. 106
- 2018, c. 8, s. 30(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-213-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 213"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further grounds

- **[[214]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-214 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  court may order the liquidation and dissolution of a corporation or
  any of its affiliated corporations on the application of a
  shareholder,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the court is satisfied that in respect of a
    corporation or any of its affiliates

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any act or omission of the corporation or any of
      its affiliates effects a result,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the business or affairs of the corporation or
      any of its affiliates are or have been carried on or conducted in
      a manner, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the powers of the directors of the corporation
      or any of its affiliates are or have been exercised in a manner

    that is oppressive or unfairly prejudicial to or that unfairly
    disregards the interests of any security holder, creditor, director
    or officer; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the court is satisfied that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a unanimous shareholder agreement entitles a
      complaining shareholder to demand dissolution of the corporation
      after the occurrence of a specified event and that event has
      occurred, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} it is just and equitable that the corporation
      should be liquidated and dissolved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternative order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On an application under this section, a court may
  make such order under this section or section 241 as it thinks fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of s. 242

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Section 242 applies to an application under this
  section.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 214
- 2001, c. 14, s. 107(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for supervision

- **[[215]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  application to a court to supervise a voluntary liquidation and
  dissolution under subsection 211(8) shall state the reasons, verified
  by an affidavit of the applicant, why the court should supervise the
  liquidation and dissolution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court supervision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a court makes an order applied for under
  subsection 211(8), the liquidation and dissolution of the corporation
  shall continue under the supervision of the court in accordance with
  this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 208
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to court

- **[[216]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  application to a court under subsection 214(1) shall state the
  reasons, verified by an affidavit of the applicant, why the
  corporation should be liquidated and dissolved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Show cause order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On an application under subsection 214(1), the court
  may make an order requiring the corporation and any person having an
  interest in the corporation or claim against it to show cause, at a
  time and place specified in the order, within four weeks after the
  date of the order, why the corporation should not be liquidated and
  dissolved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of court

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On an application under subsection 214(1), the court
  may order the directors and officers of the corporation to furnish the
  court with all material information known to or reasonably
  ascertainable by them, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} financial statements of the corporation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of each shareholder of the
    corporation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of each known creditor or
    claimant, including any creditor or claimant with unliquidated,
    future or contingent claims, and any person with whom the
    corporation has a contract.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A copy of an order made under subsection (2) shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} published as directed in the order, at least once
    in each week before the time appointed for the hearing, in a
    newspaper published or distributed in the place where the
    corporation has its registered office; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} served on the Director and each person named in the
    order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person responsible

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Publication and service of an order under this
  section shall be effected by the corporation or by such other person
  and in such manner as the court may order.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 216
- 1999, c. 31, s. 64(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of court

**[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217 .sectionLabel}** In connection with the
dissolution or the liquidation and dissolution of a corporation, the
court may, if it is satisfied that the corporation is able to pay or
adequately provide for the discharge of all its obligations, make any
order it thinks fit including, without limiting the generality of the
foregoing,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an order to liquidate;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order appointing a liquidator, with or without
  security, fixing the liquidator's remuneration and replacing a
  liquidator;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} an order appointing inspectors or referees,
  specifying their powers, fixing their remuneration and replacing
  inspectors or referees;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} an order determining the notice to be given to any
  interested person, or dispensing with notice to any person;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} an order determining the validity of any claims made
  against the corporation;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} an order, at any stage of the proceedings,
  restraining the directors and officers from

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} exercising any of their powers, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} collecting or receiving any debt or other property
    of the corporation, and from paying out or transferring any property
    of the corporation, except as permitted by the court;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} an order determining and enforcing the duty or
  liability of any present or former director, officer or shareholder

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to the corporation, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for an obligation of the corporation;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} an order approving the payment, satisfaction or
  compromise of claims against the corporation and the retention of
  assets for such purpose, and determining the adequacy of provisions
  for the payment or discharge of obligations of the corporation,
  whether liquidated, unliquidated, future or contingent;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} an order disposing of or destroying the documents and
  records of the corporation;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} on the application of a creditor, the inspectors or
  the liquidator, an order giving directions on any matter arising in
  the liquidation;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} after notice has been given to all interested
  parties, an order relieving a liquidator from any omission or default
  on such terms as the court thinks fit and confirming any act of the
  liquidator;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} subject to section 223, an order approving any
  proposed interim or final distribution to shareholders in money or in
  property;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} an order disposing of any property belonging to
  creditors or shareholders who cannot be found;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} on the application of any director, officer, security
  holder, creditor or the liquidator,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an order staying the liquidation on such terms and
    conditions as the court thinks fit,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an order continuing or discontinuing the
    liquidation proceedings, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} an order to the liquidator to restore to the
    corporation all its remaining property; and

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} after the liquidator has rendered a final account to
  the court, an order dissolving the corporation.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 217
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 108, 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 62(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-217-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 217"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of order

**[[218]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-218 .sectionLabel}** The liquidation of a
corporation commences when a court makes an order therefor.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 211
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cessation of business and powers

- **[[219]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-219 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  court makes an order for liquidation of a corporation,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the corporation continues in existence but shall
    cease to carry on business, except the business that is, in the
    opinion of the liquidator, required for an orderly liquidation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the powers of the directors and shareholders cease
    and vest in the liquidator, except as specifically authorized by the
    court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation by liquidator

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The liquidator may delegate any powers vested in the
  liquidator by paragraph (1)(b) to the directors or shareholders.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 219
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of liquidator

- **[[220]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  making an order for the liquidation of a corporation or at any time
  thereafter, the court may appoint any person, including a director, an
  officer or a shareholder of the corporation or any other body
  corporate, as liquidator of the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vacancy

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an order for the liquidation of a corporation
  has been made and the office of liquidator is or becomes vacant, the
  property of the corporation is under the control of the court until
  the office of liquidator is filled.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 213
- 1978-79, c. 9, ss. 1(F), 67
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of liquidator

**[[221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-221 .sectionLabel}** A liquidator shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} forthwith after appointment give notice thereof to
  the Director and to each claimant and creditor known to the
  liquidator;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} without delay publish notice by insertion once a week
  for two consecutive weeks in a newspaper published or distributed in
  the place where the corporation has its registered office and take
  reasonable steps to give notice of the appointment in each province
  where the corporation carries on business, requiring any person

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} indebted to the corporation, to render an account
    and pay to the liquidator at the time and place specified any amount
    owing,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} possessing property of the corporation, to deliver
    it to the liquidator at the time and place specified, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} having a claim against the corporation, whether
    liquidated, unliquidated, future or contingent, to present
    particulars thereof in writing to the liquidator not later than two
    months after the first publication of the notice;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} take into custody and control the property of the
  corporation;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} open and maintain a trust account for the moneys of
  the corporation;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} keep accounts of the moneys of the corporation
  received and paid out by the liquidator;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} maintain separate lists of the shareholders,
  creditors and other persons having claims against the corporation;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} if at any time the liquidator determines that the
  corporation is unable to pay or adequately provide for the discharge
  of its obligations, apply to the court for directions;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} deliver to the court and to the Director, at least
  once in every twelve month period after appointment or more often as
  the court may require, financial statements of the corporation in the
  form required by section 155 or in such other form as the liquidator
  may think proper or as the court may require; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} after the final accounts are approved by the court,
  distribute any remaining property of the corporation among the
  shareholders according to their respective rights.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 221
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 109, 135(E)
- 2018, c. 8, s. 31(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-221-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 221"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of liquidator

- **[[222]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  liquidator may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} retain lawyers, accountants, engineers, appraisers
    and other professional advisers;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} bring, defend or take part in any civil, criminal
    or administrative action or proceeding in the name and on behalf of
    the corporation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} carry on the business of the corporation as
    required for an orderly liquidation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} sell by public auction or private sale any property
    of the corporation;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} do all acts and execute or, in Quebec, sign any
    documents in the name and on behalf of the corporation;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} borrow money on the security of the property of the
    corporation;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} settle or compromise any claims by or against the
    corporation; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} do all other things necessary for the liquidation
    of the corporation and distribution of its property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Due diligence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A liquidator is not liable if the liquidator
  exercised the care, diligence and skill that a reasonably prudent
  person would have exercised in comparable circumstances, including
  reliance in good faith on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} financial statements of the corporation represented
    to the liquidator by an officer of the corporation or in a written
    report of the auditor of the corporation fairly to reflect the
    financial condition of the corporation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a report of a person whose profession lends
    credibility to a statement made by the professional person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for examination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a liquidator has reason to believe that any person
  has in their possession or under their control, or has concealed,
  withheld or misappropriated any property of the corporation, the
  liquidator may apply to the court for an order requiring that person
  to appear before the court at the time and place designated in the
  order and to be examined.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of court

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the examination referred to in subsection (3)
  discloses that a person has concealed, withheld or misappropriated
  property of the corporation, the court may order that person to
  restore it or pay compensation to the liquidator.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 222
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 110, 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 63(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-222-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 222"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs of liquidation

- **[[223]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-223 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  liquidator shall pay the costs of liquidation out of the property of
  the corporation and shall pay or make adequate provision for all
  claims against the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Final accounts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Within one year after appointment, and after paying
  or making adequate provision for all claims against the corporation,
  the liquidator shall apply to the court

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for approval of the final accounts and for an order
    permitting the liquidator to distribute in money or in kind the
    remaining property of the corporation to its shareholders according
    to their respective rights; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for an extension of time, setting out the reasons
    therefor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shareholder application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a liquidator fails to make the application
  required by subsection (2), a shareholder of the corporation may apply
  to the court for an order for the liquidator to show cause why a final
  accounting and distribution should not be made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A liquidator shall give notice of their intention to
  make an application under subsection (2) to the Director, to each
  inspector appointed under section 217, to each shareholder and to any
  person who provided a security, fidelity bond or fidelity insurance
  for the liquidation, and shall publish the notice in a newspaper
  published or distributed in the place where the corporation has its
  registered office, or as otherwise directed by the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Final order

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the court approves the final accounts rendered by
  a liquidator, the court shall make an order

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} directing the Director to issue a certificate of
    dissolution;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} directing the custody or disposal of the documents
    and records of the corporation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (6), discharging the
    liquidator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivery of order

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The liquidator shall forthwith send a certified copy
  of the order referred to in subsection (5) to the Director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of dissolution

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of the order referred to in subsection
  (5), the Director shall issue a certificate of dissolution in
  accordance with section 262.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The corporation ceases to exist on the date shown in
  the certificate of dissolution.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 223
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 111(E), 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 64
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-223-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 223"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to distribution in money

- **[[224]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-224 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  in the course of liquidation of a corporation the shareholders resolve
  or the liquidator proposes to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} exchange all or substantially all the property of
    the corporation for securities of another body corporate that are to
    be distributed to the shareholders, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} distribute all or part of the property of the
    corporation to the shareholders in kind,

  a shareholder may apply to the court for an order requiring the
  distribution of the property of the corporation to be in money.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On an application under subsection (1), the court may
  order

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all the property of the corporation to be converted
    into and distributed in money; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the claims of any shareholder applying under this
    section to be satisfied by a distribution in money, in which case
    subsections 190(20) to (22) apply.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 217
- 1978-79, c. 9, s. 1(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Custody of documents

- **[[225]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-225 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who has been granted custody of the documents and records of a
  dissolved corporation remains liable to produce those documents and
  records until the end of the prescribed period or of any shorter
  period fixed by an order made under subsection 223(5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who, without reasonable cause, contravenes
  subsection (1) is guilty of an offence and liable on summary
  conviction to a fine not exceeding five thousand dollars or to
  imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 225
- [2018, c. 8, s. 32](#1037671-1373941){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1037671-1373941.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-225-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 225"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[shareholder]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[226]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[shareholder]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes the heirs
  and personal representatives of a shareholder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation of actions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding the dissolution of a body corporate
  under this Act,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a civil, criminal or administrative action or
    proceeding commenced by or against the body corporate before its
    dissolution may be continued as if the body corporate had not been
    dissolved;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a civil, criminal or administrative action or
    proceeding may be brought against the body corporate within two
    years after its dissolution as if the body corporate had not been
    dissolved; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any property that would have been available to
    satisfy any judgment or order if the body corporate had not been
    dissolved remains available for such purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Service of a document on a corporation after its
  dissolution may be effected by serving the document on a person shown
  in the last notice filed under section 106 or 113.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} Service of a document on a company to which the
  [Canada Corporations Act](/eng/acts/C-1.8), chapter C-32 of the
  Revised Statutes of Canada, 1970, applied that has been dissolved by
  subsection 261(8) of the [Canada Business Corporations
  Act](/eng/acts/C-44), chapter 33 of the Statutes of Canada, 1974-75-76
  and chapter 9 of the Statutes of Canada, 1978-79, may be effected by
  serving the document on a person shown as a director in the last
  annual summary filed by the company pursuant to the [Canada
  Corporations Act](/eng/acts/C-1.8).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reimbursement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding the dissolution of a body corporate
  under this Act, a shareholder to whom any of its property has been
  distributed is liable to any person claiming under subsection (2) to
  the extent of the amount received by that shareholder on such
  distribution, and an action to enforce such liability may be brought
  within two years after the date of the dissolution of the body
  corporate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representative action

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A court may order an action referred to in
  subsection (4) to be brought against the persons who were shareholders
  as a class, subject to such conditions as the court thinks fit and, if
  the plaintiff establishes a claim, the court may refer the proceedings
  to a referee or other officer of the court who may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} add as a party to the proceedings each person who
    was a shareholder found by the plaintiff;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} determine, subject to subsection (4), the amount
    that each person who was a shareholder shall contribute towards
    satisfaction of the plaintiff's claim; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} direct payment of the amounts so determined.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 226
- 1992, c. 1, s. 57
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 112, 135(E)
- 2011, c. 21, s. 65(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-226-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 226"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unknown claimants

- **[[227]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  the dissolution of a body corporate under this Act, the portion of the
  property distributable to a creditor or shareholder who cannot be
  found shall be converted into money and paid to the Receiver General.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Constructive satisfaction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A payment under subsection (1) is deemed to be in
  satisfaction of a debt or claim of such creditor or shareholder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who establishes an entitlement to any moneys
  paid to the Receiver General under this Act shall be paid by the
  Receiver General an equivalent amount out of the Consolidated Revenue
  Fund.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 227
- 2001, c. 14, s. 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vesting in Crown

- **[[228]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-228
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 226(2) and
  section 227, property of a body corporate that has not been disposed
  of at the date of its dissolution under this Act vests in Her Majesty
  in right of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of property on revival

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a body corporate is revived as a corporation under
  section 209, any property, other than money, that vested in Her
  Majesty pursuant to subsection (1), that has not been disposed of
  shall be returned to the corporation and there shall be paid to the
  corporation out of the Consolidated Revenue Fund

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an amount equal to any money received by Her
    Majesty pursuant to subsection (1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where property other than money vested in Her
    Majesty pursuant to subsection (1) and that property has been
    disposed of, an amount equal to the lesser of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the value of any such property at the date it
      vested in Her Majesty, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the amount realized by Her Majesty from the
      disposition of that property.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 221
- 1978-79, c. 9, ss. 1(F), 70
:::

## [PART XIX]{.HLabel1}[Investigation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-111626 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Investigation

- **[[229]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-229 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  security holder or the Director may apply, [ex parte]{.otherLang
  lang="la"} or on such notice as the court may require, to a court
  having jurisdiction in the place where the corporation has its
  registered office for an order directing an investigation to be made
  of the corporation and any of its affiliated corporations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grounds

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If, on an application under subsection (1), it
  appears to the court that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the business of the corporation or any of its
    affiliates is or has been carried on with intent to defraud any
    person,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the business or affairs of the corporation or any
    of its affiliates are or have been carried on or conducted, or the
    powers of the directors are or have been exercised in a manner that
    is oppressive or unfairly prejudicial to or that unfairly disregards
    the interests of a security holder,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the corporation or any of its affiliates was formed
    for a fraudulent or unlawful purpose or is to be dissolved for a
    fraudulent or unlawful purpose, or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} persons concerned with the formation, business or
    affairs of the corporation or any of its affiliates have in
    connection therewith acted fraudulently or dishonestly,

  the court may order an investigation to be made of the corporation and
  any of its affiliated corporations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Director

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A security holder who makes an application under
  subsection (1) shall give the Director reasonable notice thereof and
  the Director is entitled to appear and be heard in person or by
  counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No security for costs

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An applicant under this section is not required to
  give security for costs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearings [in camera]{.otherLang
  lang="la"}

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} application under
  this section shall be heard [in camera]{.otherLang lang="la"}.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent to publish proceedings required

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No person may publish anything relating to [ex
  parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} proceedings under this section except
  with the authorization of the court or the written consent of the
  corporation being investigated.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-44, s. 229
- 2001, c. 14, ss. 113(F), 135(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of court

- **[[230]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-230 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  connection with an investigation under this Part, the court may make
  any order it thinks fit including, without limiting the generality of
  the foregoing,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an order to investigate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order appointing an inspector, who may be the
    Director, fixing the remuneration of an inspector, and replacing an
    inspector;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an order determining the notice to be given to any
    interested person, or dispensing with notice to any person;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an order authorizing an inspector to enter any
    premises in which the court is satisfied there might be relevant
    information, and to examine any thing and make copies of any
    document or record found on the premises;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an order requiring any person to produce documents
    or records to the inspector;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} an order authorizing an inspector to conduct a
    hearing, administer oaths, and examine any person on oath, and
    prescribing rules for the conduct of the hearing;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} an order requiring any person to attend a hearing
    conducted by an inspector and to give evidence on oath;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} an order giving directions to an inspector or any
    interested person on any matter arising in the investigation;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an order requiring an inspector to make an interim
    or final report to the court;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} an order determining whether a report of an
    inspector should be published and, if so, ordering the Director to
    publish the report in whole or in part or to send copies to any
    person the court designates;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} an order requiring an inspector to discontinue an
    investigation; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} an order requiring the corporation to pay the costs
    of the investigation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An inspector shall send to the Director a copy of
  every report made by the inspector under this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 223
- 1978-79, c. 9, ss. 1(F), 72
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of inspector

- **[[231]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  inspector under this Part has the powers set out in the order
  appointing him.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exchange of information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the powers set out in the order
  appointing him, an inspector appointed to investigate a corporation
  may furnish to, or exchange information and otherwise cooperate with,
  any public official in Canada or elsewhere who is authorized to
  exercise investigatory powers and who is investigating, in respect of
  the corporation, any allegation of improper conduct that is the same
  as or similar to the conduct described in subsection 229(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An inspector shall on request produce to an
  interested person a copy of any order made under subsection 230(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 33, s. 224
- 1978-79, c. 9, ss. 1(F), 73
:::
